ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u58	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I took part in the presentation of a report by the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs on a draft law amending the Act on Occupational and Social Rehabilitation and employment of persons with disabilities and some other laws, print No 2215, and the report of the committee is in print No 2292. At its 58th meeting on 6 February 2018, the Sejm sent the above draft to the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs for consideration. The Commission, after considering this proposal at its meetings on 27 February and 11 April this year. She gave him a positive opinion. The bill under investigation concerns the implementation of a programme of comprehensive support for families, which was adopted by the Council of Ministers in December 2016. The draft law was subject to extensive public consultation using its active forms, among others. at workshops, conferences, working meetings with interested environments, families raising children with disabilities, carers or non-governmental organisations, as well as the project was evaluated by the Joint Committee of the Government and the Local Government. At the meetings of the Social Policy and Family Committee on 27 February and 11 April, a large number of representatives of the social side were attended: employers, employees, associations of disabled people, foundations, WTCs, ZAZs, foundations and institutes. The essence of the proposed solutions is the creation of support mechanisms for families in the form of, among others. services supporting rehabilitation by introducing club classes in occupational therapy workshops, entitlement for persons insured to welfare benefits in the case of a disabled child up to 18 years old, the possibility of using flexible forms of working time organisation, including teleworking, by carers with disabilities. The amendments also concern administrative proceedings under the Professional and Social Rehabilitation Act. Certain provisions have been clarified concerning the operation of workshops for occupational therapy, obligations and rights of employers employing disabled persons and the provisions relating to the appointment of the presidents and alternates of the State Fund for Rehabilitation of Persons with Disabilities have been amended. The contested bill introduces changes to the regulation of data collected in the Electronic National Monitoring System for Disability Assessment and the reimbursement of expenses incurred by municipalities for coordination of guidance for pregnant women and their families. The draft law provides for the regulation of quotas free of deductions and executions other than those currently in force. The project includes changes in a number of laws: in the Act on Occupational and Social Rehabilitation and Employment of Persons with Disabilities, in the Act on Social Security, in the Labour Code, in the Act on Pensions and Pensions from the Social Insurance Fund, in the Act on Social Insurance in the case of sickness and maternity, in the Act on Social Pensions, in the Act on the Support of Pregnant Women and Families. It is proposed that the law should enter into force on the day following its publication, which is justified by the important interests of the State in view of its vital social importance. The draft law is compatible with European Union law. A number of amendments were tabled at the meeting of the Social Policy and Family Committee on 11 April when the bill was examined. Nine substantive amendments, several amendments by the Legislative Bureau were positively assessed. One minority request was also made in Article. Point 1 (6) (a) a draft law. The Commission, as I said at the outset, asks the High Sejm to adopt the attached bill. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u59	Thank you, Member. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would listen to the five-minute statements on behalf of the clubs and the circle. I'm opening a discussion. Please take the floor of Mrs Joanna Borowiak from the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u60	Dear Marshal! High Seym! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I would like to present my position on the Government's draft law amending the Act on Occupational and Social Rehabilitation and employment of persons with disabilities, contained in print No 2215. The draft law is closely linked to the implementation of the second stage of the comprehensive support programme for families, whose main aim is to support people with disabilities and their families. The submission is important and expected by the environment of people with disabilities, and the essence of the proposed solutions is to create further support mechanisms for families with disabilities. The project has been subject to extensive arrangements and consultation. There have been many conferences, workshops and direct meetings with representatives of interested communities. The biggest changes contained in the project relate to the functioning of occupational therapy workshops, which will be able to support their former participants in maintaining independence and independence in social and professional life by conducting club activities. The main objective of such clubs is to increase the efficiency of introducing people with disabilities into the labour market and their effective integration into society. Club activities will become a form of support also after the workshop participants have taken up work and the disabled person will be able to continue the course regardless of the loss or change of employment. High Seym! It is well known that combining parental functions with professional work requires reconciling many responsibilities. This is particularly difficult when taking care of a child with a disability when it is also necessary to combine a parent's participation in the process of treatment, rehabilitation and education of the child with a job. The project submitted responds to the expectations of parents linking their work with child care with disabilities, extending their entitlement to care allowance in the case of a child's illness until they are 18 years old also after that time, if the disability or disability are of a lasting nature. The term of care allowance will also be extended from 14 to 30 days in the case of care for a child with a significant degree of disability or a disability decision indicating permanent or long-term care. Another important change proposed in the amendment of the Act is to allow parents of disabled children to use tailored forms of working time organisation, i.e. mobile working time, flexible working time and teleworking, depending on the employer's ability and needs. This is the answer to the public expectations raised. Thanks to this change, parents will be able to plan and share their working time and childcare time more effectively. The right to use flexible forms of working time organisation will also benefit parents who care for adults over 18 years of age. The project also provides for changes in data collection in the Electronic National Disability Assessment Monitoring System and extends its scope to data on health, education, social, occupational and family status, allowing for optimisation of the disability ruling process and transparency. The next amendment to the Act aims to include funds from the Labour Fund intended to coordinate guidance and support for pregnant women and their families into the income of the municipality. This will make it possible to employ or increase the employment of family assistants and will contribute to the care of as many families as possible. Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! This project introduces a number of further positive changes in the functioning of people with disabilities in social and occupational life. It is a continuation of actions undertaken systematically by the Government of Law and Justice to improve the difficult situation of people with disabilities, measures to provide support guaranteed by the State to their families in the programme of comprehensive support for families. The overarching objective of the proposed amendments is to create effective mechanisms for the next areas of support for this social group. ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u90	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! I have the honour to present to the High House the report of the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development on the Government's draft law on supporting new investments, prints no. 2307 and 2483. This draft law was designed to adapt to the needs of instruments in the form of tax reliefs for entrepreneurs planning new investments across the country and not, so far, in special economic zones. The new regulation is the result of government work to adapt tools in the form of incentives to invest for entrepreneurs in the new economic reality, against the completely new challenges that we are currently facing. The instrument in the form of tax exemptions will now be available for new investments, which include the creation of a new production plant and reinvestments, while the conditions for obtaining public aid will depend on the rate of unemployment in the district and adapted to the size of the entrepreneur. This will apply to investments that meet the quantitative criteria for investment expenditure and to qualitative criteria in accordance with the strategy for responsible development. The draft law in accordance with the decision of the High Sejm was sent to the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development after first reading on 20 March. During the meeting in the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development on 11 April this year. an extraordinary subcommittee was set up, which I had the honour of chairing. High Izbo! Members, representatives of the Ministry of Entrepreneurship and Technology, representatives of business associations and associations and the social side were present at the extraordinary subcommittee meeting. I would like to thank all those who contributed to the report of the subcommittee in a good atmosphere once again. As a result of this meeting on 12 April this year. The subcommittee presented a report which took account of several amendments tabled by members of the Law and Justice Club. This meeting also adopted proposals for legislative and editorial amendments tabled by the Legislative Bureau of the Sejm. In turn, during the examination of the report in committee at its meeting on 8 May, an additional system amendment concerning the rules for the clearance of public aid was adopted in the sub-committee report. These changes meet the expectations of entrepreneurs, as well as the committee members who presented their need during the subcommittee. The Committee on Economic Affairs and Development, after considering this draft law at its meetings on 11 April, 7 and 8 May 2018, invites the High Sejm to adopt the proposed draft law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u91	Thank you, Member. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would listen to the five-minute statements on behalf of the clubs and the circle. I'm opening a discussion. Please take the floor of Mr Andrzej Gawron, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u92	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present the position of the club in relation to the report on the government draft law on the promotion of new investments. The project under investigation introduces solutions that aim to implement new mechanisms to support entrepreneurs in making investments across the country. It includes a number of mechanisms of tax preferences, special reliefs, which are designed to develop a favourable investment environment. This support will complement the existing solutions of the Special Economic Zones Act. The new regulations are an important instrument in the implementation of the Responsible Development Strategy. One of the elements of this strategy is to build tools that will really stimulate economic development and at the same time remove barriers to entrepreneurs. Our priority is to stimulate the growth of private investment, particularly in the areas of innovation development, technology development and exports, and to create stable jobs. The Law on the Promotion of New Investments is an update of the solutions used in special economic zones, the primary task of which was to stimulate economic growth in a specific territory of the country, e.g. through locally applied tax reliefs and exemptions. This formula is already gradually exhausted. New solutions respond to dynamically changing needs of entrepreneurs and investors, reducing investment time. At the same time, it should be stressed that we do not abandon special economic zones which will continue to operate in accordance with the rules until 2026. The coexistence of two laws will create a new system of investment incentives. The new solutions contained in the bill will include investments in the form of new production facilities and reinvestments, in line with the strategy for responsible development. The government project also envisages a complete departure from territorial restrictions on the possibility of receiving support in the form of tax preferences. The provisions of the draft law on the promotion of new investments implement a coherent investment infrastructure system and provide for legal solutions to improve the whole process, e.g. the possibility for the National Centre for Agricultural Assistance to transfer property intended for industrial and service purposes. We're going with the spirit of time. Law and Justice will support the processed project. I would like to add that as a former entrepreneur, I see huge potential in the proposed solutions. This is a very good direction, especially when all these possibilities and tools will aim to favour our Polish small and medium-sized enterprises. On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I declare one amendment to the bill. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u124	Dear Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! Thank you very much for the constructive discussion during the subcommittee and committee meetings. There was hyper unemployment, there was hyperinflation, and there was no so-called. modern know-how. So the task of economic zones at the time was to attract capital from abroad, because then there was no home capital, and job creation. The important thing is that there's work. The economy 30 years later is in a completely different place. Now we have only 6,6 unemployment. This is an indicator for the whole country. Unfortunately, in some places we still have about 15% unemployment but in other places - at 1%. And in the case of those tools that were economic zones 25 years ago, today we have completely different tasks to do. We cannot provide public aid in places where unemployment is very low, but we still need to support where it is high. So we need to diversify our tools. What's with the new bill? First of all, we're not just looking at the amount of investment. It promotes large foreign companies. Mostly these investments are near our western border. We also want to look at quality. Where there is low unemployment, the quality of this investment will be much more important, whether this investment will make the quality of the work of our Polish employees good, whether it will cooperate with universities, universities or will create a series of suppliers in Poland, whether it will develop research and development in Poland. This is the most important thing to us. We still have places in Poland where unemployment is very high. There's more to it than that. There's more to it than that. There's more to it than that. The secondary is whether a string of suppliers will be developed there, etc. So we have to do these two things with this one tool. This is the first thing we want to do. Second thing. So far, the investor, as he wanted to benefit from the economic zone benefit, had to be allowed to be there. In order not to pay the CIT tax, he had to be located in the economic zone, he had to go in there. If the zone had a geographical restriction, we had to go through the procedure to expand this zone, which sometimes lasted up to 20 months. It's unacceptable for an investor to wait 20 months for consent to enter the economic zone. So this is where we completely change our approach. It's not like the investor goes into the zone, it's the zone goes to the investor. For this reason, the whole of Poland is potentially a zone. That doesn't mean that there's a zone all over Poland. An investor, especially a small investor, because this is the most important here, SMEs or micro-entrepreneur, does not need to move from where he lives, where he works, to the zone, only the zone will come to him. So that's a lot of ease. I don't quite understand why there's a problem with local governments because we don't change anything about local governments. The law doesn't change anything, there's no centralization, there's no ownership change for zones, there's no management of economic zones. [[How can you not?]] They just get a lot bigger regions where they can help. Of course, we want it to be in the law that the zone needs to develop a development strategy for a given region, because we do not want each region to be identical. We have the Aviation Valley, we already have a automotive belt, we have specializations in Poland and we want to promote the zones together with entrepreneurs, together with universities, together with local authorities, to develop the broadly understood infrastructure according to what entrepreneurs want there, in cooperation with the management board. There is no centralization here, decisions are still made by the same individuals, by the same economic zones, so here I do not fully understand these changes. There have been a lot of questions and we will answer all the questions in writing in detail, but I will answer some at random. Mr Meysztowicz asked for a delegation with regard to the company. We've checked with the lawyers, and I can confirm that it's okay. These are State Treasury companies, not ordinary companies, so we have an answer to that. We had a question from Mr Kasprzak that there are no investments in the east, there are investments in the west. That's exactly how we want to address it. The problem is that the current Economic Zones Act is purely quantitative. These are those big foreign companies that have big capital, in one shot they employ a lot of people and have the opportunity to enter the economic zone. This is what we want to change and not only look at the amount of capital that is invested, and the number of jobs, but we want to look at and condition this quality of investment, which will allow smaller companies to invest, and smaller companies, I think, will benefit from the opportunities of the eastern part of Poland, because then we will also promote those companies which will not only put their money on quality, but will also export it, and it will export not only to the European Union, where there are 80% of our exports, but also to Asia, Africa and South America, which means that they do not have to be on the western border of Poland, near our biggest partner - Germany, they can be in central or eastern Poland. I hope that this is how we will encourage investment in other places than in the western part of Poland. I'd say, too, that so far the main task of economic zones is to sell real estate and make money on it. We want to change this character so that the main task of economic zones is to support investors to listen to the investor so that it is called. one-stop shop, the first point of contact of the investor, so that the investor doesn't have to walk around the entire local or central administration and look for a solution, only that it is the economic zone that takes on such things. As to Mrs Chmiel's question, this consultation was carried out even twice in the Joint Government and Territorial Government Commission. I think all the comments that came out of this consultation are taken into account. The honourable Mrs Nykiel asked about fossils and development. There is an amendment that has been tabled on this issue, but I think we will continue to strongly consult and consider whether there are any other options, as much as possible. Mr Cyranski - I apologize if I twisted - asked for 60 days to create this plan. It's technically 60 days, it doesn't mean the zones are waiting for this bill to come into force, and then they'll be working on it. These plans have been consulted for a long time, and work has been on them, so I am sure that these plans will be agreed in due course. Mrs Janyska also said that we are pointing out the place of investment. There's no indication here. We're just providing the investor with information that... The aid depends on the level of unemployment in a given district, so we point out that if it invests where there is high unemployment, we have more opportunities to provide public aid under EU law, and where it is smaller, we have less opportunities. It's just an indication, but we're not pointing. This is of course the absolute decision of the investor, where he invests himself and whether this place of investment is in line with his business plan. The rest of the questions will be answered in detail. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u127	Thank you, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would listen to the five-minute statements on behalf of the clubs and the circle. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Antoni Duda, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u128	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present the position of the club vis-à-vis the government draft law amending the Law on the system of conformity assessment and the Law on conformity assessment and market surveillance systems and certain other laws, prints Nos 2376 and 2442. The need to amend the Law on conformity assessment and market surveillance systems of 13 April 2016 and certain other laws, firstly, aims to bring Polish law into line with European Union law, secondly, adapts to the Polish market surveillance adopted last year to K.p.a. arrangements for the system of administrative fines and, thirdly, introduces changes aimed at facilitating the functioning of entrepreneurs, such as the indication in certain certification of accreditation of the relevant Union harmonisation legislation, the possibility to authorise the market surveillance of persons other than employees and the possibility to certify copies of documents also by an authorised person in the course of that check. Three European Union Regulations were adopted on 9 March 2016 in accordance with the new legal framework: Regulation 2016/424 of the European Parliament and of the Council on cableway installations and repealing Directive 2000/9/EC, Regulation 2016/425 of the European Parliament and of the Council on personal protective equipment and repealing Directive 89/686/EEC and Regulation 2016/426 of the European Parliament and of the Council on appliances burning gaseous fuels and repealing Directive 2009/142/EC. Poland should adapt its rules to these regulations in two main aspects. Firstly, the application of these regulations requires systemic adjustments primarily in the area of market surveillance rules. The law on conformity assessment systems for market surveillance requires changes in order to bring Polish law into line not only with the Union directives being implemented but also directly with the application of European Union regulations. The most important proposed amendment resulting from the adaptation of the Law on conformity assessment and market surveillance systems to the regulations on the conditions for the placing on the market of harmonised products is the separation under the so-called law. formal non-compliance. The draft law provides for the separation in the control and supervisory procedures governed by that law of the way in which formal non-compliance is to be dealt with. Secondly, Poland is under an obligation to lay down sanctions for infringements by economic operators of the provisions of those Regulations. This obligation is to be implemented by extending the catalogue of the provisions of the Law on conformity assessment and market surveillance systems concerning administrative fines to provisions sanctioning infringement of the provisions of the abovementioned Regulations. In order for the system of conformity assessment and market surveillance to remain consistent and non-discriminatory and for market surveillance to be effective, it is also necessary to amend the catalogue and to edit some existing sanctions in the law. When determining the level of new sanctions, sanctions already existing in the Act were laid down. The proposed draft provides for a reform of the administrative penalty procedure provided for in the amended law, bringing the rules of this procedure closer to the model of the Code of Administrative Procedure. A rule will be introduced that the accreditation certificate issued for authorisation will indicate the relevant EU harmonisation legislation in order to determine the scope of accreditation. Appropriate, i.e. one where the requirements for notification for accreditation are specified. The objective is to accelerate the examination of the results of the notification. The draft law makes a clear distinction between recognised third party organisations and other notified conformity assessment bodies. In implementing Directive 2014/68/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council of 15 May 2014 on the harmonisation of the laws of the Member States relating to the making available on the market of pressure equipment, the difference between them has not been clearly and clearly identified. However, in order for the trader to be clear which products can be made available or put into service and according to which rules market surveillance authorities will assess these products, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice amends by proposing a new wording of the Article. The 12th proposed bill I'm putting on the hands of a marshal. As a result of the amendment of the Law on conformity assessment and market surveillance systems, eight other laws, including the Law of 30 August 2002 on conformity assessment systems, are needed. In conclusion, the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will support the provisions of the revised Act and recommend that it be adopted after the proposed amendment has been adopted. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u138	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Report from the Extraordinary Commission on Deregulation on Government draft law on the management of a successful natural person enterprise, print No 2293. The Sejm at its 60th meeting on 22 March pursuant to Article. 39 (1) (a) 2 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm referred the above draft to the Extraordinary Committee on Deregulation for consideration. The contested bill concerns family businesses in the first place. The number of family businesses in Poland is very high. It is estimated that this is 60% the whole small and medium-sized enterprises sector, with around 1 million players. Family businesses account for 36% all Polish companies and produce 18% GDP. In the current legal situation, together with the death of the entrepreneur, the legal existence of the enterprise which he conducts as one which is inextricably linked to the deceased person ends. Even if there are legal successors, they cannot continue their business on the same terms What businessman owns. The heirs can only resume their activities and therefore there are many very serious practical problems. You can't use an entrepreneur's company, the problem is employment contracts and employee proxy issues. There are virtually no more civil law contracts. Administrative decisions shall expire. There is no possibility of a smooth continuation of tax settlements and the use of NIP. There are difficulties in accessing a bank account held for the business of the company. There is an obligation to reimburse State aid if it has not been fully used up to now. In turn the succession processes, which accompany such changes in the company, are, as we know, long-term and this is practically a dead time, preventing business from continuing. The problem for the entrepreneur may also be to find a successor, even if the entrepreneur takes such action, this possibility does not always exist even in the circle of the immediate family. The current legal order uses or can be used to establish a temporary manager, but this does not solve an absolute number of problems, e.g. issues of concessions, permits, use of NIP, use of the company. There is a lack of mechanism to ensure a smooth way of continuing business. Therefore, there is also a barrier for those interested in continuing the company's activities. The structure of existing companies shows an increasing trend towards the ageing of family businesses. Many companies were created in the process of market transformation of the economy in the late 1980s, hence the threat to continuing the business of these companies is a question about the stability of the economy. It is also necessary to provide legal protection for companies. The problem is that more than 30% of companies have existed for more than 20 years and 6% it exists over 30 years. The Act proposes the entire procedure concerning the succession manager: appointment and dismissal of the manager, resignation, termination of the management board, scope of business management activities in succession, any obligations incumbent on the succession manager and his responsibility, liability for obligations, as well as the possibility of using the company, the right to dispose of the funds collected in bank accounts, the rules relating to civil contracts, and the entire space associated with the transfer of rights to legal successors. Administrative obligations and any issue of business-related permits shall be regulated. The Act provides for regulations on labour law and social security. Allows you to keep an employment relationship or to resume it quickly. Another part of the regulation concerns tax law and ensures continuity in tax settlement, the use of the NIP, the use of the existing owner of tax interpretations and business continuity rules. An important objective of regulation is to protect people connected with their activities, e.g. protect the fate of contractors, co-operatives, consumers and employees of the company. The project started in 2016. A number of consultations were held and the comments submitted were taken into account in part. The consultation was attended by business organisations, including family, employers, trade unions, industry organisations. In total, more than 300 entities were invited. The work of the Extraordinary Commission has been very efficient, with the participation of the social side, including representatives of family businesses. Most of the comments submitted were legislative in nature and their introduction during the committee's work improved the quality of the proposal presented to the High House. The Extraordinary Commission on Deregulation after considering the draft Print Act No 2293 at its meeting of 12 April 2018 requests the High Sejm to approve the attached draft Law. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u139	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would listen to the five-minute statements on behalf of the clubs and the circle. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Grzegorz Piechowiak, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u140	Dear Marshal, Minister! High Izbo! It has been my honor to present on behalf of the Parliamentary Club the Law and Justice of its position on the draft law on the management of a successful business of a natural person. This is another law, alongside the business constitution, which aims to facilitate business activity and which was very expected by entrepreneurs and their families. This project was prepared on the initiative and inspiration of family business environments and was widely consulted with them. This law governs the principles of the temporary management of the enterprise after the death of an entrepreneur who, on his own behalf, carried out an economic activity on the basis of an entry in the Central Register and Information on Economic Activity and continued business activity using that undertaking. The Successive Management Act is a comprehensive regulation that focuses on intergenerational succession. It is intended to protect businesses from the consequences of the death of an entrepreneur. This project also allows to safeguard the interests of the company even during the life of the entrepreneur by the appointment of a succession manager. Thanks to the provisions of this Act, contracts, employment contracts, permits or concessions concluded or awarded by the company concerned and related to the death of the owner of the company will remain in force. In addition, the Act will facilitate tax settlement and exempt from inheritance tax the person acquiring the company. However, the most important institution of this project is the issue of the succession board, which is established in the course of life by the entrepreneur himself. If this is not the case, future heirs will be entitled to appoint a succession board and a succession manager. As I mentioned at the outset, this is a very important law for family businesses. Finally, I would also like to make six amendments, which are clear and precise. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice fully supports the draft law and will vote in favour of it. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u149	Thank you very much. And Mr Ireneusz Gains, Frees and Solidarni.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u150	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the Government's draft law on the management of the successful enterprise of a natural person, prints no. 2293 and 2454. The bill submitted regulates the principles of the temporary management of the enterprise after the death of the entrepreneur who carried out business on his own behalf on the basis of an entry in the Central Register and Information on Economic Activity and the continuation of the business activity carried out using the company. The current legal situation makes it impossible to maintain the continuity of the business after the death of the entrepreneur, it is only possible to resume the business. The procedures for establishing the acquisition of the estate and the formalities for the renewal of the legal successors of the deceased usually last for months. Such a situation poses a significant threat to the continued operation of the company and can even lead to its liquidation. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! As a legal adviser, I have dealt with cases where family companies - including those operating in the form of a civil company - could not resume their business long after the owner's death due to ongoing succession procedures. This has often resulted in the loss of confidence of counterparties and the need for job losses. All of this negatively affected the company's condition and forced the legal successors of the deceased owner to build it almost from scratch. It should be noted that these companies are the main drivers of the Polish economy. In the vast majority, they implement their tax obligations towards the State in a timely manner. The behaviour of the company on the market is important not only from the point of view of the legal successors of the deceased, but also from the perspective of contractors, consumers or workers. It was therefore necessary to develop rules that would allow companies to operate continuously after the death of the trader. The law on which we proceed gives us the possibility of succession after the deceased entrepreneur, who carried out business activities, until the legal successors of the entrepreneur and the decision on the future fate of the company are established. It lays down rules for the establishment of a succession board and general principles for the execution of a succession board. This allows a smooth continuation of the business activity previously carried out by the deceased entrepreneur without having to liquidate the company and fire employees. In our view, it is a very good law, which significantly increases the chances for the company to continue to operate and develop after the death of the entrepreneur. The Act will protect legal successors of entrepreneurs from losing their achievements, and, more broadly, it will really support the formation of a strong middle class. Indeed, it will have a very positive impact on the functioning of the whole small and medium-sized enterprises sector. The vast majority of single-person entrepreneurs affected by the draft law are part of this sector. According to data from the Central Statistical Office, coming from the REGON register, in November 2017, the number of single-member companies in Poland reached a record level of 3 million. Supporting the small and medium-sized enterprises sector is extremely important because, as I mentioned above, it helps to create a middle class that most effectively supports the economic growth of the country. History shows that countries where middle class representatives prevail are reaching a higher level of economic and civilization development. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Free and Solidarni Circle strongly supports the bill on the management of the successful enterprise of the natural person. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u158	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, it is my pleasure to present a report by the Committee on Public Finance on the Government's draft law on the amendment of the Personal Income Tax Act, the Corporate Income Tax Act and the Flat-rate Income Tax Act on certain income generated by individuals - the prints that Mr Marshal mentioned just now. After first reading on 12 April, the bill was addressed to the Committee on Public Finance, which, on 8 May, yesterday, examined the draft law and recommended that the High House adopt it. This proposal introduces a number of adjustments to the tax burden under the current rules, which are the result of an assessment of the implementation of the current rules and are aimed at clarifying them and reducing the tax burden and thus facilitating the functioning of taxpayers by merely clarifying the rules. They also aim to implement the European Commission's recommendation on minimum taxation on rental income. The main objectives of the amendment should be to enable the heirs of fixed assets and intangible assets to re-establish the initial value and thus to allow for re-imposition, which mitigates the provisions that appeared in the Act of 27 October 2017, and to allow the gifted to make depreciation write-offs not from the initial value, but at the value determined by the donor, which is the solution expected by family companies. The purpose of the government project is also to ensure uniform treatment of participants in legitimate games, run both by licensed casinos and by a state monopoly company, as well as the introduction of an income tax exemption from individuals from the proceeds of assistance granted to the Police by persons who are protected by it, where, through the information they provide, they participate in the disclosure of offences. To this day, the Act exempts from taxation remuneration from the provision of assistance to state services paid from the operational fund. On the other hand, as regards contact with the police, these issues raised some doubts, they required clarification, as has been done in this project. The catalogue of areas where 50% of revenue costs can be used has also been extended - among others. in the field of construction engineering, translation, computer games. In this respect, an amendment was tabled to extend the catalogue of these privileges to research and development, scientific and teaching activities and teaching by academic teachers and teachers of secondary schools. However, this amendment was not accepted by the members of the committee. 12 Members voted in favour, 25 against and no one abstained. Special attention is paid to the benefits of the Law relating to the exemption from taxation of interest waivers on housing loans so-called. An old wallet. The repayment of housing loans of an old wallet, otherwise called Balcerowicz's loans, was regulated by the 1997 Act. This was the case for loans up to 31 May 1992. This law on State aid in the repayment of certain housing loans, among others. A transitional repayment of interest on these loans from the State budget has been introduced. This was a form of compensation for the huge burden which fell on borrowers because, firstly, interest was charged on interest, i.e. interest capitalization was carried out even though the payments were timely and although the borrowers did not agree at the time of the conclusion of the contracts and the interest was commercialised contrary to the provisions of banking law. This resulted in a huge burden on some borrowers who, paying their loan for many years, made payments equivalent to two or even three times the value of the apartment. This is a very painful case in Polish housing, in Poland, because a very large number of borrowers, members of housing cooperatives suffered a huge injury in the form of an inexplicable usury. We in previous terms have tried twice to amend the law to reduce these burdens, for example by otherwise charging this aid from the State: to charge interest, not interest, but capital itself, which would cause that capital would not create this interest again. However, this test was not accepted by the majority at that time, which we regret very much. The 1997 amendment introduced the Law on State aid in the repayment of certain housing loans. Both the temporary-purchased interest and the capitalised interest were allowed to be remitted to the burden of the State budget. This law states that if borrowers have paid or paid off their loans regularly for 20 years - this also applies to individuals and housing cooperatives, starting on 1 January 1998, but not later than 1 January 2006. - it is after a timely, regular repayment for a period of 20 years that are guaranteed by the Law of redemption into the burden of the state budget precisely those charges arising from the temporarily redeemed interest and capitalised interest. These are large amounts, please, because even for 2018 for this purpose was reserved in the state budget of PLN 390 million. In turn this burden is measured per member of the cooperative and on average ranges from several hundred PLN to more than 100 thousand. zł. There is also a large burden - in the order of up to PLN 1 million - on housing cooperatives. These are the amounts of redemptions. Since, as of 1 January 2018, the most massive use of this privilege began - a provision for members of housing cooperatives who meet these conditions - a problem arose. On the one hand, these persons are guaranteed by the interest waiver law, but on the other hand there has been a problem with the tax on this redemption. The banks have observed to cooperatives, as well as to members of housing cooperatives, that under the current law, which is true, this is a taxable advantage. And that's where the problem was solved. I am very pleased and thank the finance minister that, after a thorough examination, after an initial attempt to solve the problem only through the regulation at the moment, because this was not possible by the bill, comes up against this issue, i.e. those who meet the conditions for redemption after 20 years of repayment from the State aid law, since the entry into force of this law which we are discussing today, may not be afraid to tax the benefits of this redemption. Thus, it is possible to apply for the State aid law, which leads to a write-off of interest. Once again, I stress that this is a very important thing, because it raised doubts, a certain social concern. Of course, there are other doubts, e.g. what about those persons who, although they were legally entitled to redemption, fearing the consequences in the form of taxation, continued to pay off those redemptions. I hope that the Ministry of Finance, together with other ministries, will proceed as soon as possible to clarify and reassure these members of housing cooperatives and cooperatives themselves and we will solve this problem together. Of course, we are ready to work, to cooperate in this direction. These are actually the most important issues that the bill raises. There is another important issue, because in the face of the aggressive struggle to keep them hidden, mainly by large economic operators, taxes on the rental of large real estate areas, has been introduced by the current finance resort minimum tax. The European Commission raised some reservations about this, suggesting that this income tax should cover all real estate, buildings, excluding residential buildings which have been put into use under general government programmes. It is therefore recommended to apply a minimum income tax to those properties or parts thereof that are rented. This also required an adjustment in the Act on income tax on natural and legal persons, which was made. Therefore, in favour of the need for these solutions which the Act implements and in favour of resolving the problems it is facing, after the discussion that took place yesterday, the committee explicitly supported the adoption of the bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

As a result of the proposed changes, the minimum income tax will cover all buildings located in Poland, with some exemptions. On behalf of the Polish Free and Solidarni Circle, I present my position on the Government's draft law amending the Personal Income Tax Act, the Corporate Income Tax Act and the Flat-rate Income Tax Act on certain income generated by individuals (prints 2291 and 2291-A). The draft law is moving towards tax rationalisation of economic operators' behaviour in a competitive environment. The doubts raised in the discussion of this amendment at the meeting of the Committee on Public Finances were mainly related to the extension of the catalogue of entities subject to this tax and to burdensome administrative obligations for smaller entities. There have also been reservations about the efficiency of managing the rental of very small areas, such as ATMs or distribution machines for beverages and foodstuffs, in the context of the contested law and the mechanism for applying for tax returns in the event of a real loss on the rental activity of the property. The procedure for agreeing with the European Commission on the non-discriminatory nature of the law also raised doubts. The Ministry of Finance has in substance justified the provisions adopted and agreed that the problem of very small areas can be resolved by excluding these revenues from the Act. Other reservations concerned the extension of the catalogue of professions covered by tax concessions resulting from cultural, scientific and technological works, which are extremely important for the development of the country. In particular, the discussion focused on the professional group of primary school teachers and computer graphics. In our view, the provisions adopted describe creative fields of activity well. The Free and Solidarni Circle welcomes the government's initiative to introduce a number of tax modifications in this project. They are fundamental in nature and meet the economic and social needs of our country in a systematic manner. It impresses the number of changes introduced systemically in many areas of life. The regulations concern, among others, the following: taxation of winnings obtained in slot games, extending the exemption of income from financial assistance received from the Police, increasing the catalogue of revenue obtained from the use of copyright, introducing favourable depreciation rules for family businesses, clarifying the revenue generated from the sale of shares acquired under incentive schemes, introducing exemptions related to residential loans, clarifying the rules on taxation of monetary contributions to companies. ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u184	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! As I was just saying, this bill was created in connection with the voices of local governments and teachers. They pointed out that the current provisions of the Educational Law in at least two elements had some inconsistencies, or at least different interpretations. The first of these suggestions concerned the composition of the competition committee and the manner in which it was appointed. Let me remind you that the Education Law here provides for representatives of the governing body, i.e. self-government, and the supervisory authority, i.e. the curator, as well as representatives of parents, teachers and trade unions. This last piece... Although the amendment of the Education Law, which was so fundamental several months ago, tried to regulate the matter unambiguously, indicating that the various representative trade unions should have one representative on the competition committee, it turned out that the provisions of that law are interpreted differently. Although this intention was accepted by the legislator in most local governments, in some local governments it was the case that trade unions, which are a federation, were replaced by the principle of one representative for a representative trade union and there were representatives of two, three trade unions, which were part of a federation trade union. The case was so controversial that in one of the cities it was sent to a voivodship administrative court, which accepted the position of the voivodship - which was such as the legislative position - that one representative should be chosen from individual representative trade unions. However, the case is still pending before another judicial instance, and some local authorities are still trying to apply such an interpretation that they can identify several representatives of these smaller trade unions that are part of the federation. We felt that, in order to regulate this matter, it was worth submitting a bill which would clearly confirm the will of the legislator, expressed several months ago, formulated in a slightly different way, and would achieve the same goal, so that there would be no question of interpretation. Hence those changes which concern Article. 63. 14. The second part, as I mentioned, concerns the teaching board's rules of procedure. These were, above all, the voices of teachers who signaled that when decisions were taken at the meeting of the pedagogical board concerning the management positions at school - for example, expressing their views on whether the term of office of the Director should be extended or whether the election of the vice-director of the school or the institution and similar subjects - some directors unfortunately move to impose a public vote on the pedagogical board, which clearly makes it difficult to express the true intentions of the teachers participating in the vote because of concerns, for example, about how the vote against the current director will be dealt with. Hence, we were asked to make this clear. The point is that there is no need for a public vote to be taken again on a secret vote in personal matters, only in order to make it clear in the rules. Indeed, we have proposed such a provision that explicitly forces the educational councils in schools to apply this solution, and this has generally not raised any doubts. We hope that this problem will be resolved in this way. We welcome the position of both the Sejm Analysis Office and the majority of social organizations that they agreed to both of these solutions. As I said, there were some doubts about union representation. We, in agreement with those comments which were made during the committee's meeting, will propose amendments to the Rule. In order to amend the content of this section on trade unions and to make this content the following: In order to conduct the competition, the school or institution shall appoint a competition committee in its composition, and in point 3 on trade unions it shall read as follows: one representative of the trade union organisations within the meaning of the Council of Social Dialogue Act, comprising the school or establishment in which the competition takes place, selected from among the members of their organisational units or the organisational units of the trade union organisations which are members of the representative organisations. [[♪ The bell ♪]] a trade union association of teachers, the representative of which may not be employed in the school or institution to which the competition relates. In addition, there are two other amendments: one concerning the transitional period and the other, in principle, editorially covering this amendment of the regulations not only schools but also educational establishments. ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u218	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The proposed amendment is linked to two issues. The first is the introduction of secret ballots. It is a question of voting on the resolutions of the pedagogical board on matters relating to persons acting as head of the school or the opinion of candidates for such positions. In their explanatory memorandum, the applicants argue that there have been times when school heads put pressure on the pedagogical board and forced transparency of the vote, e.g. as regards the opinion on the extension of the term of office of the school principal. This led to a kind of intimidation of workers who, fearing for their positions or positions, voted as expected by the director. The new rules introduce the mandatory secrecy of voting in such cases, with the aim of free and objective decision-making by the members of the Board. We are able to accept this change because in our opinion it serves the good of the teaching staff. Even if the argument is a little out of line, it works according to the principle: the guarded Lord God protects. Another change proposed by the applicant is no longer so clear and obvious. This is to clarify the regulation contained in Article. 63. 14, concerning the participation of trade union representatives in the competition committee in the competition for the position of school head. The proposed new wording of the Article. 63 The Law of Education undoubtedly clarifies the question of the participation of representatives of the trade union organisations in the competition committee in the competition for the position of head of the school, but at the same time excludes from the committee one of the representative trade unions active in education, i.e. ZNP or Trade Union Forum. According to the article added. 63. 14a These associations will be obliged to appoint a joint representative for the composition of the competition committee. We therefore maintain and fully support the proposal presented by the Polish Teachers' Union. We express the need to supplement the wording in the paragraph. 14a by adding after the words "involved" in the same word. The amendment proposed by the NAP will enable one representative to be selected from individual representative organisations, not from a joint candidate. Our position will be expressed during the vote. Thank you very much.

The point is that they will be appointed at the request of the National Judicial Council, the composition of which has been formed in the assessment of many who are not in conformity with the existing constitution, and not in the evaluation of politicians, but of experts who have been involved in this area for years. We're gonna face this as a result, and you're gonna have to be aware of that too, with doubts as to whether the court's assessors are really them. And it is not because of the qualities of these persons or the mode of their appointment that I would like to point out from this point of view, but because of the mode of their appointment and the composition of the body which will ask for their appointment. That's the question. The same doubts will apply to newly appointed judges. Another element discussed in the bill is the package of amendments to the emergency complaint. The scope of the case and the temporal scope of the emergency action have not been changed, from eight to two, but the number of entities through which the action can be brought to the Supreme Court within 3 years of the validity of the law. Since 1997, i.e. since the current constitution, several hundred million cases have been brought to the Polish courts. It's not a slip of the tongue. You've heard that number well. The Supreme Court recognizes between 10 and 12 thousand on average. cases per year, maintaining the average time for the case to be dealt with until the judgment is delivered at the level of 7 months. If he comes to this court about 100,000. cases, apart from other cassation and cassation complaints, are judges of the Chamber of Extraordinary and Public Affairs, a chamber which has not even been established yet, will recognize them for decades. It's also worth thinking about today. How it works today The Constitutional Tribunal, we already know, twice as slowly, because it is an organ affected by your reform. An emergency complaint should concern only criminal matters and not proceedings of all possible types. In the case of civil and economic matters, it is a fight for legal trade. The certainty of legal trading in Poland, also in the opinion of many, is on the line. That's what I'm talking about. Therefore, only in the first quarter of this year a considerable sum of PLN 150 billion evaporated from the Warsaw Stock Exchange. An instrument of social justice badly chosen may not ensure justice, nor the speed of its execution, nor increase our economic security. In Poland, the so-called. a narrow model of constitutional complaint, i.e. only the provision of the law on the basis of which the judgment was given, but not the very judgment. An emergency complaint after all these changes actually introduces the next type of constitutional complaint, this time in a broad model, which occurs e.g. in Germany. This is not only against our constitution, but will also create problems with the subsidiarity principle required by Article. 35 The European Convention for the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms, which states that the European Court of Human Rights can only deal with the matter once all the remedies provided for by internal law have been exhausted. PSL-UED does not accept such legislative powdering as a prescription for improving the rule of law in Poland [[♪ The bell ♪]] And this bill cannot count on our support. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u253	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Circle of the Members of the Free and Solidarnia, I present my position on the report of the Committee on Justice and Human Rights on the draft law amending the Law on the Supreme Court and certain other laws (Prints No 2480 and 2526). The bill submitted concerns the introduction of amendments to four laws, which in Polish legislation are crucial for the proper functioning of the justice system. We are talking here about the Law on the System of General Courts, the Law on the National Judicial Council, the Law on the National Judicial School and the Public Prosecutor's Office and the recently passed Law on the Supreme Court. The amendments to the first three laws are basically a revision of the procedure for the appointment of judicial assistants. The President of the Republic of Poland shall appoint the President of the Republic of Poland at the request of the National Judicial Council for an indefinite period, as proposed by the applicants. In the current legal state, the Minister of Justice has the competence to appoint the assistants. In view of the systemic position of the head of the State, applicants have reformed the procedure for appointment of assesors also as regards the possibility for the National Courts to object to individual candidates for these posts. Such an objection will be possible at the stage of the procedure prior to the appointment of the President, not as it has been now, after the appointment of the Minister of Justice has been made. This change should be assessed positively, in particular with last year's attention, which is particularly detrimental to the appointed assessors to the exercise of this power by the National Judicial Council of the previous term of office. As regards the amendment of the Law on the Supreme Court, applicants propose the reform introduced by the Act of 8 December 2017 of the institution of emergency complaint. First of all, the Article has been amended. 89 indicating the reasons for the complaint. In line with the proposed wording, an emergency complaint can be lodged where this is necessary to ensure compliance with the principle of a democratic rule of law that implements the principle of social justice, which is the general principle of the system described in Article. 2nd Constitution of the Republic of Poland. In the current wording, the legislator has used the premise of the need to ensure the rule of law and social justice, which - with which it is difficult to argue - due to the lack of solidity in Polish Judaism and doctrine could cause serious interpretation difficulties for the Supreme Court. Another significant change in the scope of the emergency complaint concerns the limitation of the circle of entities empowered to lodge an emergency complaint against decisions which have become valid before the entry into force of the draft law in question. On the basis of the added provision of Article. 115 § 1a From such judgments, an emergency complaint will be granted only to the Prosecutor-General and to the Ombudsman. It is worth noting that in other cases, i.e. with regard to final decisions taken after the entry into force of this proposal, the other bodies listed in Article will have the power to lodge a complaint as well as so far. 89 § 2 Act on SN, i.e. President of the Public Prosecutor's Office of the Republic of Poland, spokesperson for the rights of the child, spokesperson for the rights of the patient, chairman of the Financial Supervision Commission, financial spokesman and President of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection. The amendment also clarifies the possible decisions of the Supreme Court in situations where the contested action caused irreparable legal effects, but it has not been five years since its validity, or if the annulment of the decision would violate the international obligations of the Republic of Poland. The proposed draft amendment to the Act on the Supreme Court also provides for changes in the procedure of the President of the Republic of Poland's consent to the further occupation by the judge of the Supreme Court after the age of 65. As proposed by the applicants, the President is required to consult the National Judicial Council, which shall forward this opinion within 30 days of the President's request to present that opinion. In drawing up an opinion, the National Judicial Council shall take into account the same conditions as in the similar procedure concerning the general judiciary, i.e. an interest in justice or an important social interest. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The project submitted as a whole should be assessed positively. It is hoped that no changes to the Supreme Court Act will be necessary for a longer period. We must finally allow this law to function so as to be able to really assess whether it has fulfilled its most important objectives and objectives. Once again, I would like to point out that the process of changing the justice system has not been completed. In addition to the changes that we have been working on at the current Sejm meeting, there is a need to improve procedures that affect the speed of proceedings. This refers in particular to civil procedure, but also to matters relating to economic law, labour law or criminal procedure. I am pleased that the Ministry of Justice is working on the reform of civil and criminal procedures. We look forward to the final results of this work in the form of ready-made legislative proposals. The Free and Solidarni Circle will support this bill.

If I do not hear opposition, I will think that the Sejm has accepted the offer. I can't hear you objecting. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u255	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, This is an important moment for the whole academic world in Poland, because the final phase of a unique not only Poland, but also the whole European Union's process of dialogue between the government and the academic community, the dialogue which I believe will be the final one - the adoption of what has been called a constitution for science, the new law in a systematic way that regulates the functioning of Polish universities or scientific institutes. When in autumn 2015 the government included the camp of the United Right, Polish universities were in the implementation phase of the previous, adopted in 2014, another, nth in a row, revision of the Law on Higher Education. In the academic world, it was common to believe that any subsequent amendment only deepens chaos, leads to the fact that the document, the content of the law becomes in many respects illegible even to the authors of these changes. In short, it was common in the academic community to expect that we would not once again correct the document as many times as it had already been amended, but to start working on a new law. This is also the decision I made in autumn 2015 and I immediately announced that the work on this law will be conducted through dialogue with all interested professional groups: from outstanding scientists, through young capable doctors, doctoral students, students, administrative staff at the university. The starting point of this law was the competition, within which we selected three teams, teams consisting of representatives of academic circles, teams which prepared three competitive concepts of detailed assumptions for the law. From the beginning, we wanted to collide with each other various arguments, different points of view and try to find a golden center between them. In parallel to the work of these teams, we launched a series of dozens of great conferences, but also dozens of different kinds of symposiums and discussions, and the culmination of this process of dialogue on the new shape of the law was the National Congress of Science, which took place in September 2017 in Krakow. I would like to thank everyone very warmly - many thousands of scientists, doctoral students, students, university administrators who took part in this unique process of dialogue, a unique process of seeking new legal solutions. I would like to say that this uniqueness of the way to investigate the new law appreciates very many critics, the vast majority of the critics of the final solutions. This uniqueness was also recognised by the European Commission. I probably belong in this Chamber to those who, rather than to a greater extent, attach importance to the position of the European Commission, but I would like to say that the European Commission has repeatedly put this process of creating a constitution for science as a model for all EU countries with all kinds of major systemic changes, and that there is such a major systemic change in the case of a constitution for science. As I went to work on the bill, from the beginning I said that we were betting on a few vectors. The first and the basic is the vector. increase the autonomy of universities. In the Polish academic world, among Polish scientists, didactics, students have huge potential, which is often looped with too detailed regulations, too narrow scope of academic freedom. A measurable example of this expansion of autonomy is the volume of the new law. Firstly, it is a single law that replaces the four existing ones. Secondly, the overall volume of legislation is twice as low as the number of rules currently in force. A few years ago, I proposed to introduce into the Polish legislative system a principle which was introduced in some European countries at the time, led by the United Kingdom: they in, they out, means to stop this endless expansion of bureaucratic regulation by applying the principle that if one new rule is to be introduced, it is at least one of the previous ones with a similar scope - out. Since then, many years have passed, at that time the United Kingdom has introduced a different rule: they in, two outs, to bring into force one new rule, namely: they in, are at least two of the previous ones - outs. I would like to say that the constitution for science fully meets this principle. The number of regulations will be halved, more than half the number of regulations will be reduced. This is the measurable space to increase academic autonomy. A much larger area of decision-making will now be shifted from law and regulation to external acts of universities, i.e. above all statutes. An example of this increased autonomy and, at the same time, some of the misunderstandings that accompany the Act is the issue of internal structure. As you know, under the law today all Polish universities must divide into faculties. Meanwhile, we all know great universities in different regions of the world that do not divide into departments. They share, for example. For schools, they're divided into colleges. In short, the internal structure of the best universities in the world is very diverse. And with us, there's one shamel, that's a post-peerelic remnant. The new law gives universities autonomy in terms of how they will define their internal structure: whether they will divide into departments, so far, probably the vast majority of universities will choose such a path, or decide to divide into schools, colleges or adopt mixed structures. This solution, as I mentioned, is linked to a misunderstanding, namely that there was a charge that the law would remove departments from the top. Nothing more wrong. The Act removes only the compulsion to structure itself into departments, and thus significantly extends the freedom of the academic community, which itself will determine, by adopting the statute, what this internal organisation is to look like. Another example of such a very measurable and very important extension of autonomy for universities is the change in the model of university funding. At the moment, they're funded by grants, so each zloty is top-down by the ministry. Universities have their hands tied when it comes to making the most effective use of these budgetary resources. In the new law, we replace the grant with a subsidy, so it is the university that receives a certain amount of money from the state budget that will decide how to use it. Increasing the autonomy of universities must be more responsible. Where there is greater freedom, there must be greater responsibility. In other words, we need to change the model of management of Polish universities. It is no secret that the current model is extremely inefficient. In the new law, we want to significantly increase the powers of single organs, i.e. above all, the rector. As in the government, it is important that the Prime Minister has a strong position, as should the real leader, the real leader, the leader with not only the authority of the law, but above all the authority of democratic elections, because the rectors in Poland are elected by the academic community. However, if we are to increase the competence of the rector, then it is necessary to create a body that will monitor the daily management of the university. This is one of the more controversial elements of this law, namely the establishment of a new body, which is the university council. I would like to draw the attention of those of you who may share the concern that the establishment of a council in some way violates the autonomy of universities, that such councils work successfully in more and more European Union countries, and one of the recommendations of the European Commission to Polish universities was the introduction of these advices. Councils will be 100% elected by the academic community. That I would like to emphasise very strongly: 100%. There is no space for external interference, in particular political interference. One member of the board will be elected from among themselves by students - this will be the chairman of the student council - the other will be elected by the Senate. In fact, the Law imposes only one limitation on the choice of council members. More than half of the board members must be non-university members. Why would you do that? Well, the council is an organ that has two very important missions. Firstly, as I mentioned, it is to monitor the work of the university authorities, and secondly, it is to be a transmission belt between the university and the socio-economic environment, in short, between the university and our society and Poles. Universities must not succumb to the temptation to be an ivory tower, universities should serve society. I was a scholar at Cambridge University, and when I got there, I saw the motto of this college. This motto does not apply to the fact that, I do not know, to seek the truth or even to serve the interests of professors or the interests of the academic community. The motto is to serve the society, serve society. For this reason there are all universities, including Polish universities, and certainly each of these universities is trying to serve the public, but thanks to the daily presence of external representatives at universities this dialogue between universities and society will certainly be better. The head body of the university - I want to emphasize this very strongly - remains neither the rector nor the council. Moreover, the Council will not have any powers in terms of research or education, which is the essence of each university's mission. This chief organ will remain the Senate. The Senate, among others. he will adopt the statutes of the university, i.e. something like the constitution of the university, he will adopt the rules of study, he will give an opinion on the development strategy. In short, it will deal with everything that I have just mentioned and what is part of the university's mission, i.e. the training of students at the highest level and the conduct of research at a competitive level vis-à-vis the best countries in Europe. We certainly can afford this, especially in terms of intellectual potential, but I believe that in time, soon, also in terms of material, financial potential. I would also like to say that when choosing the rector, we also retained this principle of internal autonomy and the principle of collegiality. The Rector can be chosen in two ways. One is foreseen [[♪ The bell ♪]] in the present law, although it has never been applied. I mean, the way of the contest. I understand Mr. Marshal will give me a little more time.

That's why we're changing the rules of granting doctoral or habilitation rights. In fact, today there is a purely quantitative criterion, namely the number of independent academics - professors or habilitated doctors - employed at a given university. Unfortunately, this results in the fact that the habilitation is often given by academic centres, departments or institutes in which research has not been carried out for a long time, departments which receive the lowest category, category C, in the context of the once every four years of categorization, i.e. evaluation of scientific achievements. Therefore, in order to improve the quality of PhDs and the quality of habilitation, we want to link the right to confer degrees with the scientific category, i.e. with the quality of scientific research grown in a given discipline at a given university.
