As the first Mrs Joanna Borowiak, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u257	Dear Marshal, High Seym! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice in relation to government draft law on higher education and science, in print No 2446, and the Law on Higher Education and Science, in print No 2447. The projects submitted aim at consolidating, standardising and simplifying basic legislation in the field of higher education and science. For the sake of clarity, it is important that the provisions laid down in the various laws so far are contained in a single legal act. The draft Law, which introduces the Law on Higher Education and Science, is a consequence of new regulations and aims to implement them effectively and efficiently. The aim of the reform of higher education and science including changes in the functioning of the system, management, financing and evaluation of the quality and activities of universities are: systemic problems in the field of higher education and science, their negative impact on the quality of research carried out by Polish universities and scientific institutions, the level of education of students and PhD students, and the lack of focus on funding rules, evaluation of scientific activities and career development on the achievement of scientific excellence. In a group of key problems affecting higher education and science in Poland, alongside the level of funding, there are examples of flawed principles of organisation and system of universities, which limit the efficient management, mismatch of the structure of the higher education system to social and economic challenges, limited financial autonomy of universities, unsatisfactory quality of higher education, insufficient level of matching of education to the needs of the socio-economic environment. For example, in the Human Capital Balance Study conducted in 2009 and 2014, under high youth unemployment conditions, more than 3/4 employers pointed out that there was no possibility of finding workers with the right competences. Another problem is the low effectiveness of doctoral training. In the information on the results of NIK's audit of January 2016 in the field of doctoral education, we find the conclusion that these studies are not a fully effective way of training researchers. Studies are being massaged, especially in social sciences and humanities. Although the number of post-2008 PhD students has increased significantly, as nearly 1/3 and in the case of stationary doctoral studies by 2/3, the average number of doctoral jobs defended is barely more than 40%. , and the number of doctoral degrees awarded remains constant at 6,000. per year. If Poland takes the tenth place among the 33 Member States of the Organisation for Economic Cooperation and Development, according to the indicator of their completion - already fourth place from the end. The Supreme Audit Chamber also expressed doubts about the quality and effectiveness of teaching in doctoral studies due to their easy availability and mass training of PhD students, some of which are not prepared to start a scientific career. Of the 261 students studied by NIK, only 108 from 2013 and 2014, or approximately 41%. He's got a doctor's degree. Another identified problem of higher education and science is the system of degrees and titles which inhibits the pursuit of scientific excellence and the conduct of interdisciplinary research. In 2012, the number of young researchers systematically decreased. In the October 2017 assessment of NIK, this is due to inadequate motivational mechanisms, the recruitment of researchers mainly in connection with teaching work and the absence of certain minimum requirements for candidates to the degree of habilitation. Between 2012 and 2016, the number of academics with a doctoral degree and without a doctor's degree decreased by nearly 6%. They also decreased their participation in the number of all scientific workers. Another problem is the low level of relevance of research results conducted in Poland in world science, including the low level of internationalisation of Polish science. For example, Poland's net contribution to the programme in 2014 and 2016 in the EU research programme Horizon 2020 has exceeded the resulting funds by more than PLN 2 billion. Among the most important changes relating to the provisions of the Law to the diagnosed and presented problems are changes in the rules on the organisation of the institution and the relationship between its bodies, changes in the structure of the higher education system, changes in the financing rules of the higher education and science system, changes in the quality and relevance of higher education, the introduction of a new model of doctoral training and the application for a doctoral degree, changes in the classification of scientific and artistic disciplines and the system of degrees and titles and, finally, changes in the principles of evaluation of the quality of scientific activities. The proposed law provides for the regulation of the minimum salary of the assistant, assistant, professor and professor, the adjustment of the hourly salary of teaching and research and teaching staff, as well as the increase of the salaries of academic teachers and their dependence on the average salary in the economy. The problem of decommunization of Polish universities is an important issue that has long been waiting for a solution. After the end of World War II, universities were not neutral, strictly reserved for learning the field of activity, but became an important element of the communism and vassalization of the Polish state. Higher education requires a moral rebirth in this area. The Act submitted provides for the settlement of the academic environment with the period of the Polish People's Republic. Those who have worked in or cooperated with the PRL security bodies will not be allowed to sit in the higher education institutions or representative institutions. According to the POL-on register of higher education institutions, the project affects 398 universities, including 260 non-public universities. The law contains solutions most frequently advocated by experts and academic community. It responds to identified problems, to the need to improve the quality of education and research. Mr. Marshal! High Seym! Science and higher education obligations and their impact on the social and economic development of Poland are described in detail in the Responsible Development Strategy for 2020 (with a view to 2030) adopted by the Council of Ministers on 14 February 2017. The implementation of the objectives of the strategy requires changes in the higher education system and its reform has been qualified as a group of key projects. The proposed law is a tool to implement one of the priority objectives of the Responsible Development Strategy, which is to improve the quality of human and social capital, among others. by reaching the value of R &amp; D expenditure of 1,7% GDP by 2020 and up to 2.5% GDP in 2030 Systemic changes in the area of organisation, education and funding rules for higher education are needed and undisputed and the projects submitted contain interesting solutions. Various voices and opinions of higher education and science environments shall be covered with regard to the proposals put forward. We want to listen to them and address them. Mr. Marshal! High Seym! ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u259	Mr. Marshal! Prime Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform has the honour to present its position on draft laws on higher education and science law and regulations implementing the Law on higher education and science. The law on higher education and science is an act of major importance not only for the functioning of higher education and science, but also for the development of the country, containing important and extensive legal regulations. However, there is no doubt that a well-functioning legal framework in this area should be accompanied by a significant increase in spending on higher education and science. Without it, even the best legal solutions will not produce the expected results. Very often, our universities and research institutions are accused of being low in various rankings. However, if we compare expenditures on our education and on our science, it will turn out that the effectiveness of these expenditures is at least decent. If e.g. We're going to put together the research and the number of patents, and it's going to turn out that we're not very far off in these sets. The bill was created as a result of relatively long-term work by various bodies: three teams prepared preliminary projects, a team was set up to support the Minister directly, a series of conferences devoted to the various spheres of higher education and science functioning. The project was presented by the Minister of Science and Higher Education himself during the National Science Congress. The project has gained acceptance of parts of the environment, although it must be stressed that numerous votes are, however, negatively evaluating the reforms. A number of solutions are positively assessed, including: harmonisation of the funding stream of universities. Here I agree with the Minister that it strengthens autonomy. The scholarship system for PhD students, doctoral leave, studies and a new idea are also highly assessed. You can also appreciate the withdrawal from some bad solutions, such as: the form of establishment of the university. I have no doubt that it should be a statutory form. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: Article 9 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 of the project we read that the university is autonomous on the principles set out in the Act. It is interesting why the sound of the current one, which I would like to recall, has been abandoned. Today's provision is as follows: The University is autonomous in all areas of its activities on the basis of the principles set out in the Act. In the light of the rules contained in the project, the implementation of the principle of autonomy of universities raises doubts. In an open letter of professors of Polish scientific centres to parliamentarians of the Republic of Poland, concerns are expressed, and even the belief that the law will eliminate the autonomy of universities and that there will be liquidation of academic self-government and the threat of authoritarianism. A great deal of concern from this point of view arises from the project, alongside the existing bodies of public universities, i.e. the rector and senate, the university council. According to art. Article 19 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 the university board consists of 6 or 8 persons appointed by the Senate and the head of the student council, with more than 50% of the university board members. The Council is to ensure appropriate contact with the environment. This must certainly be assessed positively, also according to today's wording on monitoring financial economy, management, but the university board has a very broad competence beyond what I have already indicated. Her tasks include: the choice of the rector or the appointment of candidates for the rector after the Senate's opinion. And here we can say that the university council in the proposed composition is a waiver of the principle of autonomy of the university, even if we assume that it is elected by the Senate. Against the background of this regulation, there may be a situation that the rector of the university will be a person imposed on the academic community, that it will be decided by non-members of this community. After all, autonomy means the right of the collective to settle their internal affairs. Serious concerns about the functioning of the university council are formulated in the open letter of professors of Polish scientific centres already quoted by me. Here it is indicated that it will operate in Poland under conditions which differ from European standards of democracy and the rule of law. On this basis, concerns are expressed in this letter. There is no doubt that the university council should not have the choice of the rector. The appointment of candidates for the rector could take place, but only if they are accepted by the Senate, not only by opinions. The Act introduces a far-reaching freedom, including in the case of public universities, in shaping the internal organizational structure of universities. Many doubts are raised as to whether such a break-up with our academic tradition will promote the smooth management of public universities. It seems that the maintenance of basic organisational units with deans and faculty councils is justified. We would not then have a controversial solution giving the senate the power to grant degrees. You could imagine regulation in this way: regulation is traditional, but there is a rule that allows for derogations. It's not a good idea for a university professor after a doctorate for teaching. Abolition of the requirement of habilitation and the possibility of becoming a professor on the basis of teaching achievements can lead to the maintenance of weaker or weak staff at universities. Current solutions for spending on higher education and science are much less favourable than in previous versions of the law. I'm looking at how much time I have left.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u269	Dear Marshal! Prime Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the club of the Polish People's Party - Union of European Democrats, I am pleased to present my position on the governmental draft law on higher education and science, print No 2446, and the government draft law introducing the Law on higher education and science, print No 2447. Ladies and gentlemen! Recognizing that the pursuit of the truth and the transmission of knowledge from generation to generation is a particularly noble activity of man and recognizing the fundamental role of science in the creation of civilization - such words begin the act presented by Deputy Prime Minister Jarosław Gowin, Mon. Constitution for science. These beautiful sentences, beautiful names, however, may be misleading, because according to academic teachers, the proposal of the Ministry is rather a disaster for science. More than 250 people, professors, people of science, have signed an open letter in which they admit, write, quote: the project, although it is not without good proposals, such as the universal scholarship system for PhD students, the elimination of endless doctoral studies, parental leave for PhD students or easier management of university funding streams, but these good elements of this puzzle cannot obscure the basic winding-up mechanism included in this project. The most important problem is that the law will eliminate the autonomy of the university, subjecting all elements of the very subtle social fabric of the academic world to one person - the rector. We see in this solution the liquidation of academic self-government and the threat of authoritarianism. We are also concerned about the threat of one-sided dependence of free scientific research on the state and private patronage. These are serious reservations, which are partly agreed by experts from the Parliament's analysis office, because it turns out BAS has issued a crushing opinion on the bill, stressing that after the entry into force of the law many smaller universities will lose the rank of universities. The Bureau warns that the Act proposes adverse changes to the system of evaluation of scientific quality for universities in smaller cities, followed by less powers and less funding. It may turn out that by law universities in cities such as Kielce, Lublin and Białystok will lose the rank of university. Paradoxically, the party, which is named as a member of the United Right camp, which until recently carried the slogan on banners about equalising Polish A and B opportunities, will contribute to this law to deepening differences, to the disadvantage of Eastern voivodships. There could be more problems. From the comments which, after reading the project, impose themselves as key, unacceptable, I will mention a few here. The first one concerns the position of the rector. The project envisages over-strengthening the position of the rector by centralising powers and concentrating power in one hand. The president is to be included, among others. the representation, management, preparation of a draft statute and other internal acts, human resources policy, in particular as regards the appointment and dismissal of internal heads of units, evaluation of the work of teachers and the possibility of releasing them. Such a solution is a dangerous departure from proven and established solutions that democratized the life of universities, facilitated the smooth management of diverse units, departments, institutes by selected persons, representing scientific disciplines conducted by the individual, although they still did not protect many universities from the problem of concreteisation of staff, the existence of interest groups, the fight against internal competition, etc. Focusing the full power in one hand can effectively deepen such pathologies, as the rector will receive tools to facilitate the creation of his policy, and it cannot be assumed that it will be free of particular interests and only serve the good of the university. It is also worrying that there is no regulation on the ability to control the rector, assess his work or appeal during his term of office. According to the solutions adopted, even a weak rector, e.g. At risk of loss, loss of prestige, reduction in the number of students, he will be able to perform a function, and with favorable internal arrangements, which with such broad power he will be able to ensure, he has a chance of re-election. Interestingly, the almighty rector could theoretically share working time between different universities, because there is no ban on the rector's work in other universities. This could lead to a serious conflict of interest. Interestingly, the Act is to extend the terms of the current Rectors - theoretically to prepare universities to implement reforms. However, the problem may be that, after the end of the term of office, no one will be responsible for the solutions adopted, because with their entry into force, the creators will cease to act as rectors. Unfortunately, it's a mix-up. There are serious doubts about the departure from the faculty structures - the Institutes - to the idea that the university is a basic organisational unit managed by the rector and evaluated as a whole. Such a solution may have a number of negative consequences, e.g. lack of internal competitiveness, motivation to take ambitious action, including research. The system will still be feudal and inefficient. There's no clear information. About the two-time thing. It should definitely be eliminated at doctoral level, as the work of such people in two universities is extremely unfavourable for each of these universities. A scientific worker, who focuses on the development of teaching salaries, has essentially no time or strength for development, research, foreign internships or innovation. In addition, taking up two jobs, it often blocks jobs and the ability of universities to build their own staff, especially smaller ones. Another important issue is the issue of state higher vocational schools. These for 20 years have enabled thousands of people to acquire higher education or to improve their qualifications by conducting other forms of training. For many cities and regions, they have become important centres to stimulate the development of human and economic capital. In our view, therefore, public higher vocational schools should generally function as teaching institutions, but with the possibility of carrying out research, in particular related to local needs and a newly defined mission; they should retain the right to conduct second degree studies, especially as many of them have excellent facilities for conducting them; they should have the possibility of introducing an extended educational offer so that they can become regional training centres of competence desired by employers, confer professional qualifications, confirm and certify qualifications. With regard to the institution's system, a mechanism must also be followed - a change in the senate's setting-up mode and scope, which should be the opinion-making body represented by all groups of employees and students. It should have competences similar to those proposed for universities. Ladies and gentlemen! The Rector should be selected by a convention extended to representatives of academic teachers and other university staff, students and trade union representatives. It should have a strong managerial position and be responsible for the development and finances of the school and limit employment to only one university. The role of the university convention as a specific supervisory board should be strengthened as regards the Chancellor - this function should be focused on economic governance and administration. The priority should be to strengthen the education process, not to demolish the structure of universities. Today, there is not enough time for innovative forms of education and new courses of multidisciplinary studies. These are directions that should also be addressed by the Ministry of Science. These are just some of the reservations that arise from the analysis of the project presented by the Ministry. The Club of the Polish People's Party - the Union of European Democrats believes that reform is needed, but the question is whether it is in this shape. These talks should continue, especially since the law presented by the Ministry of Science is a kind of phenomenon among the PiS-Polish. I believe she was the only one consulted, Prime Minister, and hence the appeal to you to continue along this road. The change should be with the agreement of the parties concerned, Minister, show your colleagues in the government how to consult draft laws. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u338	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The draft law aims to bring Polish law into line with the solutions set out in Commission Regulation 2016/403 and to implement certain provisions of Directive 2015/719 of the European Parliament and of the Council into Polish law. The provisions of the Regulation shall apply directly in the Member States of the European Union from 1 January 2017 and the implementation of the provisions of Directive 2015/719 in the national legal order of the Member States should take place by 7 May 2017, except that by 27 May 2021 Member States are to adopt specific measures to determine which vehicles or combinations of vehicles in use are likely to exceed the maximum permissible mass. The most important changes planned include the categorisation of serious infringements of road transport obligations and conditions: the most serious infringements with the NN symbol, very serious infringements with the BPN symbol and serious violations with the NSA symbol. This will allow the automatic exchange of information between Poland and the other Member States of the European Union under the ERRU system as regards serious infringements of EU rules and their qualifications, which may lead to the loss of good repute by road transport operators - the requirement of good repute is essential for the occupation of the carrier. New solutions assume that a serious infringement, a very serious infringement or the most serious infringement may be committed not only by the road carrier but also by the transport manager or by the driver. The initiation of a procedure for the fulfilment of the requirement of good repute shall be subject to the frequency of infringements in accordance with the rules laid down in Commission Regulation 2016/40. Another amendment is the repeal of the criminal liability of the person managing the transport or other road transport activities and the introduction of the administrative responsibility of that person. The aim of the amendment is to improve competitiveness in road transport and ensure equal treatment between national and foreign operators by making it possible to control and collect charges from foreign operators. In the current legal state, criminal proceedings prevent the actual punishment of foreign entities. The third amendment is to clarify the existing rules for checking compliance with road transport obligations and conditions and to initiate administrative proceedings to meet the requirement of good repute. Fourth amendment - clarification of the existing rules on the functioning of the National Electronic Register of Road Transport Entrepreneurs, in which, among others, information is collected on serious infringements of EU rules and their qualifications, which could lead to the loss of the good reputation of the road carrier. In order to implement certain provisions of Directive 2015/719 of the European Parliament and of the Council, the definition of intermodal transport and shipping operations has been added to the Road Transport Act. An obligation has been introduced for the consignor to draw up and forward to the road carrier a declaration specifying the weight of the container and the replacement body as determined in accordance with the actual state. Introduced for road transport operator carrying out intermodal transport operations, i.e. the carriage of cargo using more than one means of transport, the obligation to present, at the request of the competent inspection authority, a declaration specifying the weight of the container and the replacement body as determined in a manner consistent with the actual state. If these obligations are not complied with by the consignor or the road carrier carrying out the intermodal transport operation, a specific monetary penalty is provided for. A provision imposing an obligation on a provincial inspector to plan and organise checks on vehicles or combinations of vehicles in such a way as to cover at least 5% the total number of checks on road transport carried out in each calendar year. The proposed law introduces additional amendments which go beyond the scope of the implementation of European Union law. These are among others. the following amendments to the provisions of the Law of 20 June 1997 on Road Traffic Law. A provision is added which provides that a non-normative vehicle with an authorisation which does not correspond to actual technical parameters is a non-normative vehicle without authorisation. A provision is added which provides for the possibility of imposing a penalty of a kind of administrative decision for breaching the rules on non-normative vehicle piloting and determining the amount of that penalty. The responsibility of the carrier shall be clearly regulated. The provision concerning the required non-normative vehicle authorisation for the carriage of a combination of vehicles consisting of more than the permitted act is also clarified. The issue of the removal from the road of a foreign vehicle of an entity established in a country with which the Republic of Poland is not bound by a mutual enforcement agreement is also regulated. To sum up, the proposed changes are expected to contribute to better supervision of the transport services market in Poland. In addition, they will effectively eliminate unfair competition between carriers and ensure greater protection of the internal market against unfair competition from non-EU carriers. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u342	Dear Marshal! High Seym! On behalf of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club, I present the position of the club vis-à-vis the draft law amending the Road Transport Act and certain other laws contained in print No 2459. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, The objective of the revised Road Transport Act is to adapt the current regulations to the requirements of Union law resulting from Commission Regulation No 403 of 18 March 2016 laying down the types and gravity of individual infringements of Community law. To this end, the government project provides, in particular, for substantial amendments to the three annexes to the Road Transport Act and in the Annex to the Dangerous Goods Transport Act, as well as for a change in the form of liability of certain entities for violations of obligations and conditions of carriage by road. The main flaw in the proposed rules on sanctioning infringements is that a particular infringement can be borne by the driver, the transport manager and the transport operator. This is unacceptable. The liability should be borne by the entity that contributed to the infringement. Punishment for the same violation cannot be duplicated. To date, the imposition of a penalty is in violation of our law, i.e. only in cases where a specific person, a particular employee, has been found guilty. This regulation was developed in 2011 in cooperation with the interested environments with Members of all policy options. Today, it is proposed to impose administrative penalties, so without investigating the involvement of the blamed person. It is enough to state that there is a breach of the law. Consequently, a worker who has no impact on the infringement may be punished and the imposition of a penalty may also result in a loss of professional qualifications by the worker, which is carried out automatically in practice in connection with other provisions. And, for example, the worker may lose his right to work as transport manager for a period of one year. The proposed amendment has nothing to do with the implementation of Union law. It shall be introduced only to facilitate the work of the inspection services. I would like to remind you: I was very firm in presenting my club's position against the proposal to place toll collection on the roads in the Road Transport Inspection because of the burden of duty of this service. Now this flawed regulation the government is trying to help, of course, at the expense of entrepreneurs. Furthermore, the amount of the fines should be proportionate to the seriousness of the infringement. Growth proposed in the revision not more than 100% is difficult to accept because there is no rational justification. There is no mention in the justification for the bill on this subject. The current rules on penalties for infringements of the rules governing road transport and the level of sanctions are the subject of a compromise which takes into account the principle of fairness. As the practice points out, they have led to a real reduction in the number of infringements. Unless, High Izbo, the proposed regulations are a desperate search for financial resources in the pockets of road transport operators following an easy distribution of money from the state budget. Therefore, my club's position on this project will be presented after work in committee, during which, I hope, we will improve this very harmful project. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u345	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! I present on behalf of the Free and Solidarni Circle a position towards the government draft law amending the Road Transport Act and some other laws. The proposed amendments result from the need to adapt Polish legislation to EU requirements. The commitments were imposed on us by Commission Regulation (EU) No 2016/403 of 18 March 2016 relating to the classification of serious infringements of Union rules which could lead to the loss of good repute by the road carrier and amending Annex III to Directive 2006/22/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council. The proposed amendments to the Road Transport Act will ensure that the control powers of the Road Transport Inspections are aligned with the existing Union rules governing the construction and operation of recording equipment used in road transport. The above cases will be dealt with, after the revision, in Article. 4. The Law of 20 June 1997 proposed road traffic law, among others. changes that will make the journey of any non-standard combination of vehicles required to be authorised by category VII. Where the authorities authorised to check the absence of an authorisation of an appropriate category, III-VI, the penalty shall be imposed as if it were not authorised. This will be governed by the article. For the purposes of this Regulation, the following definitions shall One of the changes that will be made in the Act of 19 August 2011 on the Carriage of Dangerous Goods will be the arrangement of the infringement system. Their description has been clarified and the amount of fines provided for them has been adjusted according to their categorisation. It is envisaged to amend the provisions of the Act indicating the distinctness of checks on compliance with the obligations of carriage of dangerous goods by means of enabling the competent minister to define a separate model for the control protocol for such carriage, to highlight the distinctiveness of the infringement of administrative proceedings and the autonomous nature of the decision imposing a penalty. In addition, the Act will amend administrative fines for infringements relating to the carriage of dangerous goods by road. This is due to the need to carry out an appropriate gradation of those penalties in relation to the category of infringements provided for in Regulation (EU) 2016/403. In view of the change of responsibility of transport managers or other activities relating to transport from criminal to administrative traffic, it is necessary to introduce a rule that the provisions of the proposed law applicable to acts committed by those persons before the date of entry into force of the proposed law will continue to apply to them, so that they will continue to be subject to criminal liability in this respect. This shall be governed by Article. 8. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: The deadline has been set for the transmission to the Chief Inspector of Road Transport by the Chief Police Officer, the Head of the Border Guard, the Head of the National Tax Administration and the Provincial Road Transport Inspector of the first aggregated information concerning the control of the masses of vehicles or vehicle combinations referred to in the draft Article. 90a. In addition, a deadline has been set for the first transmission by the main road transport inspector to the European Commission of data relating to the checks carried out on the masses of vehicles or combinations of vehicles, no later than 30 September 2020. This proposal clarifies the existing rules on checks on compliance with road transport obligations and conditions and provides for appropriate amendments to the Annexes to the Road Transport Act and the Dangerous Goods Transport Act of 19 August 2011. Changes are necessary due to EU legislation and therefore the Free and Solidarni area supports the proposed law. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u346	The draft amendment eliminates unfair competition between carriers and provides greater protection for the internal market against unfair competition from non-EU carriers. According to the project, the so-called category was categorized. serious violations of obligations and conditions of road transport: the most serious infringement - NN, the very serious infringement - BPN and the serious infringement - NSA, which will allow the automatic exchange of information between Poland and EU countries. New solutions assume that a serious infringement, a very serious infringement or a serious infringement may be committed not only by the road carrier and the transport manager, but also by the driver. In contrast, the initiation of proceedings to meet the requirement of good repute will depend on the frequency of infringements. The draft provides that when intermodal transport is carried out, the consignor will have to draw up and forward to the road carrier a declaration stating correctly the weight of the container or swap body. On the other hand, the road carrier will have to present a declaration at the request of the authorised inspection body. A new type of responsibility for transport managers - from criminal to administrative - should be considered important. The maximum fine for the driver after one check will be 2 000. zł. ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u349	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! The draft law on amending the Law on Liquid Biocomponents and biofuels and some other laws actually refers to the government's draft establishment of the Low Emissions Transport Fund. The draft of this law is to indicate the beneficiaries of this fund and what the fund has transport tasks in Polish cities. The fund is to finance zero- and low-carbon transport in Polish cities. The beneficiaries of this fund will be entrepreneurs, local authorities as well as research and education institutions. The first funds of this fund will be mobilised in 2019. The funds from the Low carbon Transport Fund will provide real support to Polish producers. Entrepreneurs will be able to finance, among others. investments in the production of alternative fuels, tests and implementation of innovative solutions for developing new alternative fuels or innovative design solutions and the construction of a network of electric vehicle charging stations or filling both liquefied gas and frozen gas or hydrogen. In this way we want to use the potential of Polish scientists, research centres and entrepreneurs to build a new branch of Polish industry. This will create a system in which scientific and research agendas will work widely with business, among others. through funding from the research fund and implementation of ready-made solutions. Electromobility and the alternative fuel sector are an expression of our aspirations to create business and technological solutions in Poland, which will constitute a competitive counterweight in our country. Under this fund we plan to finance the purchase of tens of thousands of electric cars together with the construction of electric car charging infrastructure in Poland in the number of about 20 thousand. charging points throughout the country. In 2019, aid for buying an electric car may already amount to around 25 000. Evil on the vehicle. In the following years, the level of support will depend on the needs of the customers and the development of the market. The funding for the construction of charging points may amount to up to 5% of the cost of building such a point in the first period, but as with the support for the purchase of a vehicle, we will respond flexibly to the signals from the market regarding the need to increase or reduce support for these investments from public money. At the same time, we maintain our readiness to join the fund with funds of about PLN 1 billion in the production of electric cars in Poland. Our goal is to actively participate in industrial projects that will determine the competitiveness of the Polish economy in the coming decades. These activities are intended to encourage drivers, companies and local authorities to support the use of zero- and low-carbon modes of transport. The effective effect of these initiatives will be to reduce smog. Therefore, money outside entrepreneurs will go to local governments, which in particular during the autumn-winter period are faced with a problem of low air quality. Social research shows that more than half of Poles consider smog to be a serious threat, identifying the transport sector as the main cause of air pollution and looking at environmentally friendly vehicles to address this problem. The government agrees with Poles on this issue, which is why a project for the establishment of the Low Carbon Transport Fund has been prepared. The government strongly responds to the expectations of Poles about improving the quality of life not only in the largest cities, but also in smaller centres. In the development of electromobility, smaller cities, with a population of several thousand, were also involved at the very beginning. This shows the level of government's aspirations for modernisation and we want to support these aspirations. It is also worth pointing out here the cost of air pollution, both material and incalculable for money, that is, human. The expenditure of the National Health Fund itself for the treatment of respiratory diseases, respiratory and thoracic malignancies amounts to over PLN 3 billion per year, PLN 3.5 billion. So within two years, the fund spends more on this than the 10-year budget of the Low Carbon Transport Fund. It is also estimated that about 45 thousand. people die in Poland every year prematurely because of diseases caused by smog. I would like to stress that the proposal to set up the Low Emission Transport Fund should be seen as a whole, not only with the Act on Electromobility and Alternative Fuels, but also with the draft Law on the Quality of Solid Fuels, as a certain regulatory package. The individual heat sources, the low-quality fuel they often use and the transport sector are two main sources of air pollution. Therefore, the only way to combat smog effectively is to solve the problem in these areas in a comprehensive way. These laws create a package for clean cities prepared by the Ministry of Energy in cooperation with other resorts. This package for clean cities gives us real regulatory and financial tools to fight smog, which will soon provide Poles with the air quality they expect for themselves and their children. I would like to stress that the funds for this fund will come from an issue fee of PLN 8 and will be divided: 15% for the Low carbon Transport Fund and 85% to the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management, for all tasks related to environmental activities, the fight against smog, to reduce emissions and in general to promote air cleanliness. I would also like to point out, because there have been allegations that this charge will be an additional social cost and that it will increase the price of fuel in the market, to stress that, ladies and gentlemen, year by year in 2016 alone, fuel sales increased by around 16%. , and the comparison of 2016 to 2017 - 2016 is a new regulation sealing the VAT system and all payments in general, the circulation of money in terms of fuel sales - is an increase in fuel sales by 21%. This resulted in a significant increase in the profitability of fuel companies, those which determine the wholesale price of fuel - among others. I can state that Orlen's profit for 2017 exceeded PLN 7 billion - and for this reason, because of the objective in which they want to participate, and the purpose in which they want to participate, is also the fight against smog, which causes fuel, these companies, the management boards of these companies decided that due to the introduction of this charge they would not raise the price of fuel, agreeing to a small reduction in the profitability of their own companies. I stress this after the discussion, after the declarations of the management boards and after our meeting today at a conference related to the Polish Chamber of Liquid Fuels, which at this conference clearly stressed the desire to take part in the fight against emissions. So for this reason, I am authorized at this point, taking into account the declarations which have been given to me to declare in the presentation of this law that this law will not result in an increase in the price of fuels on the market. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

On behalf of the club PSL-UED, I have the honour to present my position on the draft law on ratification of the Agreement establishing a European Molecular Biology Laboratory. The purpose of the Act is to enable the President of the Republic of Poland to ratify the international agreement establishing the European Molecular Biology Laboratory, which was drawn up in Geneva on 10 May 1973. The European Molecular Biology Laboratory (EMBL) is an international research organisation of European countries. The institution shall conduct basic research in molecular biology, training of scientific personnel at all levels, provide scientific services to Member States and actively engage in technology transfer. The European Molecular Biology Laboratory for the quality of its research is among the seven best scientific institutions in the world. The ratification of the international agreement will allow Poland to change its status from a prospective member to a Member State. This means that Poland will receive one vote in the Council of the European Molecular Biology Laboratory (EMBL Council). We will therefore have a real impact on the institution's budget and its research programme. Polish scientists will also be able to use the state-of-the-art research infrastructure located in laboratory resources. It is also an opportunity for new channels to attract partners to international research projects. Ratification of the agreement will strengthen the chance of Polish scientists to employ at different levels in the European Molecular Biology Laboratory. The presence of Poland in the European Molecular Biology Laboratory will ensure continuous access to knowledge and experience in molecular biology and will allow technology transfer on the same principles as scientists from other Member States today. It will help to raise the level of Polish science in this field, while stimulating the socio-economic development of the country. Finally, Poland is the last of the Visegrad Group countries to join the European Molecular Biology Laboratory. High Seym! The PSL-UED Club will vote in favour of the law on ratification of the Agreement establishing a European Molecular Biology Laboratory.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u422	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The aim of the proposed law, print Nos 2304 and 2394, is to enable the President to ratify the international agreement concerning the European Molecular Biology Laboratory, which is an international research organisation of European countries specialising in research in the fields of natural sciences. It currently includes 23 Member States, three forward-looking countries, including Poland, and two non-European associated countries. EMBL is ranked at the top of the ranking of scientific institutions in Europe and is among the seven best in quality research in the world. Around 1/3 of EMBL's research groups received prestigious ERC grants, namely the European Research Council. Poland, becoming a member of EMBL, will be among the decision-makers of the organisation, and only the Member States have the right to decide on the shape of the budget and the research programme, and each country has one vote. By becoming a fully-fledged, equivalent member of EMBL, we will gain access to technology transfer rules and the possibility of creating spin-off companies, access to the organization's acquis and therefore knowledge and experience of 23 Member States. Polish scientists will gain access to excellent research infrastructure, the best bioinformatic resources in the world, specialized equipment from the top shelf, which can push our biological and genetic research forward for years. This will also help to open up new labour markets. Member States have an impact on the development of pan-European initiatives such as projects of European large research infrastructures, which potentially gives the opportunity to attract partners in international scientific projects and to provide high-level training, doctoral programmes and post-doctoral trainees, and even efforts to create a joint doctoral programme between selected universities and EMBL. The acquisition of EMBL membership will allow Polish researchers to participate in research, advanced education programmes, but will also increase the prestige of their work during recruitment, and when recruiting for EMBL, candidates from Member States have priority. All this will stimulate the social and economic development of our country. From Central European countries, the Czech Republic joined EMBL in 2015. Hungary, which declared its readiness to join at the same time as Poland has already ratified the agreement, is completing this process. Poland received a letter informing about the adoption of the Resolution of the EMBL Council on 29 July 2017, while being informed about the need to initiate the ratification procedure and the activities to be carried out in this regard. From the Visegrad Group countries Poland will be the last country joining EMBL. If we want Poland to be recognised as leading in this region, we must also ensure that this is where efforts are focused towards establishing an EMBL partner laboratory in the future. Covering contributions and participation in 2018 would be provided within the limits of the resources available in part of the budget 28: Science, which is the responsibility of the Minister responsible for Science, without the need to increase it. In the following years, the costs of the contribution will be financed within the limits of the expenditure of Part 28 of the budget: Science as set out in the Budget Act for a given year without the need to raise additional resources for this purpose. It is modern in favour of the rapid ratification of the Agreement establishing the European Molecular Biology Laboratory, drawn up in Geneva on 10 May 1973. We'll vote in favour.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u445	High Seym! On behalf of the PSL - ED Club, I have the honour to present a position vis-à-vis 12 draft laws, of which 10 agrees that the Polish President will terminate the mutual support and protection agreements between the Government of the Republic of Poland or the Polish People's Republic and the governments of the following countries: the Republic of Greece, the Kingdom of Spain, the Republic of Finland, the Republic of Bulgaria, the United Kingdom of Great Britain and Northern Ireland, the Republic of Austria, the Republic of Lithuania, the Republic of Hungary, the Republic of Croatia and the Kingdom of Sweden. The eleventh draft law concerns the approval of the Sejm by the President of the Republic of Poland to terminate the agreement on mutual support and investment protection between the Polish People's Republic and the Federal Republic of Germany, as amended and supplemented by a subsequent Protocol between the Polish Republic and the Federal Republic of Germany on the same matter. The 12th bill concerns ratification of the Agreement between the Government of the Republic of Poland and the Government of the Republic of Latvia on mutual support and investment protection. What is the link between all these draft laws? They all concern agreements with the Member States of the European Union and were concluded before Poland entered the EU. Their aim was to attract foreign investors to Poland and guarantee them the security of their investments through the possibility of resolving disputes outside the Polish legal system. After Poland's accession to the EU and the adoption by our country of the acquis communautaire, the need for a specific legal guarantee of the security of investments located in Poland has lost its justification. Moreover, the European Commission considers that these agreements may be incompatible with EU law and therefore calls on all the governments of the EU Member States that have concluded such agreements to resolve them. One of the main arguments of the European Commission is that the agreements in question - because of excessive protectionism and the possibility of redress against an international investment tribunal - may infringe the Treaty rules on the movement of capital, freedom of establishment and freedom to provide services. The European Commission also considers that these agreements, called intra-EU BITs, are also contrary to the fundamental principles of EU law, such as the principle of primacy, unity, efficiency and certainty of EU law, and the settlement of disputes through international investment arbitrage appears to be contrary to the exclusive competence of EU courts. It is difficult to disagree with this argument from the European Commission. It is also shared by the Polish Government, which further argues that the termination of the above agreements will not have negative social consequences, on the contrary, it should be expected that it will be welcomed by public criticism in Poland of intra-EU BIT agreements and international investment arbitration. The current government of the Republic of Poland does not even see the need to maintain an agreement on mutual support and investment protection with the Government of the United Kingdom of Great Britain and Northern Ireland, which is, as we know, in the process of leaving the EU. In its explanatory memorandum to the bill on termination of the agreement of 8 December 1987, the government of Prime Minister Mateusz Morawiecki states that the termination of the Agreement should not affect the economic relations between the Republic of Poland and the United Kingdom, and announces that Poland will also seek to ensure that possible agreements concluded by the EU and EU Member States in connection with the Brexit provide a high level of protection for EU investors. In written justifications up to 12 projects submitted, the government informs the High Sejm that it has conducted consultations with national economic authorities. However, despite some reservations on their part regarding the elimination of the possibility of resolving disputes through international investment arbitration, the government ensures that Polish investors very rarely benefit from this form of protection provided for by intra-EU BIT agreements (only three such cases have been reported by the Ministry of Entrepreneurship and Technology). The government of Prime Minister Morawiecki argues that Poland bears high costs of arbitration proceedings and calculates that since 2006 it was about PLN 53 million. Taking all these arguments into account, the PSL Club - ED will vote in favour of the 12 draft laws that are the subject of this debate.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u447	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I ran, so I apologize for the short breath, but I hope I didn't disappoint the High House's work too much. The basic objective of the bill is to align Polish law with the provisions of the Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council on tachographs used in road transport and amending the Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council on the harmonisation of certain social provisions relating to road transport. The proposed regulation improves road safety by limiting the manipulation of data recorded by the tachograph concerning the working time of the driver, introduces new powers for the control services and prohibits the production, distribution, advertising and sale of equipment for the manipulation of these data, and clarifies the requirements for the servicing of tachographs. The proposed changes should make it safer and enforcement of road transport rules should be more effective. The draft law on tachographs reduces the risk of misuse of tachographs by defining new operating conditions for the installation, repair and verification of tachographs. Currently, two generations of tachographs - analogue and digital - are covered by two separate legal acts: the Law on Measurements and the Law on the System of Digital tachographs. The new draft harmonises and covers all requirements for both generations of tachographs in a single legal act. The proposed regulation harmonises and clarifies how to proceed when programme or hardware modifications are made in the tachograph's operating system. In addition, it is assumed that a single permit may be issued for service activities of all types of tachographs. The ability of carriers and operators whose activities may affect the safety of the tachograph system has been excluded. It was assumed that in the event of a reasonable suspicion of connection of an unauthorised tachograph device, an inspection officer, e.g. The Road Transport Inspection will be able to direct the vehicle to an approved workshop to check the possible connection of such a device. The draft law also regulates issues related to the issue of digital tachograph cards. There are currently four types of cards on the market: drivers, companies, workshops and controls. The draft law introduces the possibility to issue temporary and non-renewable driver cards in justified cases for a period not exceeding 185 days. This is a great facilitation for drivers employed by Polish carriers who do not have a permanent residence in the European Union. The draft law also sets out penalties for infringements of EU law and regulation. It was also proposed that the President of the Main Office of Measures should extend the authorisation to carry out activities in the field of analogue tachographs to entities which do not meet the new requirements of hiring a workshop technique. An extension of the authorisation will be possible for a period not exceeding 2 years from the date of entry into force of the Act. The aim is for these actors to have sufficient time to employ technicians who will be properly trained and pass the test. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u449	Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! We are launching a debate at the first reading of the draft law on tachographs. It is therefore worth defining what a tachograph is. The name of this device comes from Greek: tachos, which means speed, and grapho, which means: write. The tachograph is a recording device in the time function run by the vehicle, as well as its instantaneous speed. The Tachograph also records the activity of the driver, i.e. periods of his driving work, waiting time for work, i.e. on-call time, as well as rest. Modern devices can also save other parameters, e.g. engine rotation. Tachographs can be analog and digital. As the Minister mentioned, in the Polish legal system at the moment these provisions are contained in two laws. One of them regulates the issue of analogue tachographs, the Law on measurement, and the other - the issue of digital tachographs. This bill provides that we will have one regulation for tachographs. Tachographs as on-board equipment were introduced for road safety reasons. Most countries - including all countries of the European Union - have introduced limited working time standards for drivers. Why? Therefore, for drivers to sit behind the wheel of the vehicle rested so that it is a guarantee that their psychophysical condition is a guarantee of road safety. But since there are standards that limit the working time of drivers, these standards need to be enforced. And that's what tachographs do. However, it turns out that these devices can be introduced into the state of counterfeiting of data. The regulation we are talking about is to guarantee a state in which the manipulation of tachographs will become impossible. We will set up a system for the control of tachographs, installation control, repair control, so that security is guaranteed throughout the European Union in the common market. Another aspect of the use of tachographs can also be pointed out, as these standards restricting working time are also social in nature and tachographs also allow enforcement of legislation in this area. In the European Union, tachographs were introduced by legal standards in December 1985 and in the summer of the following year this was already implemented. These provisions, which are included in the draft in print No 2458, are standards adapting Polish law to the European Union regulation. This implementation deserves support. The details will be discussed on [[♪ The bell ♪]] a committee meeting. It is worth noting that, although these interferences in tachographs are recorded by the Road Transport Inspection, they are not so many, because according to the justification that has been provided - if your marshal permits, I will quote these data - last year the Road Transport Inspection detected about 270 cases of connection of an unauthorised tachograph device, which caused data distortion, and about 380 cases of arbitrary interference with tachograph work. It is worth adding that in Poland we have registered 34 633 companies authorised for international transport in the countries of the European Union, and these companies totaled over 218,5 thousand. vehicles. That means the amount of manipulation is insignificant. But we need to bring this size to zero for security reasons. Thank you for your attention. ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u458	Thank you very much, Minister. The discussion is closed.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u459	The proposed regulation leads to the implementation of EU Regulation 165/2014 on new construction solutions for tachographs and reduces the risk of misuse. In addition, the regulation also prohibits advertising and sales of tachograph manipulation equipment. Currently, issues related to the operation of workshops servicing two generations of tachographs are governed by separate laws: the Law on Measurements and the Law on the System of Digital tachographs. The introduction of a new law will put all the necessary solutions in order in one piece of legislation. Requirements for workshops carrying out installations, repair and inspection of tachographs and certification of technicians have been defined. It should be pointed out that there are important issues concerning the clarification of how to proceed when programme or hardware modifications are introduced in the tachograph operating system. It was proposed that, in the event of a reasonable suspicion of connection of an unauthorised tachograph device, the appropriate services should be able to direct the vehicle to an approved workshop in order to verify the possible connection of such equipment to it. The draft law sets out penalties for infringement of EU law and regulation. There is a ban on the production, distribution, advertising or sale of equipment or objects designed to manipulate tachographs. A fine, limitation or imprisonment of up to 2 years will be punishable for violating this prohibition. PSL - UED is in favour of further work on the project.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u460	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I am pleased to present the position of the Poselski Club on the government draft law on tachographs included in print No 2458. Ladies and gentlemen! The draft tachograph bill submitted by the Government to the High House is intended, as we read in the explanatory memorandum, to align the legal status of the Republic of Poland with Regulation (EU) No 165/2014 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 4 February 2014 on tachographs used in road transport and repealing Council Regulation EEC No 3821/85 on recording equipment used in road transport and amending Regulation (EC) No 561/2006 of the European Parliament and of the Council on the harmonisation of certain social provisions relating to road transport. According to the government, the proposed regulation increases the efficiency and efficiency of the tachograph system and ensures better enforcement of social legislation in road transport, and Regulation No 165/2014 is binding in its entirety and directly applicable in all Member States. High Izbo! Currently, there are two types of tachographs on the market. These are older analogue tachographs and newer digital tachographs. At the same time, the issues related to these two different technologies are governed by two different laws: the first, of 11 May 2001, the Law on Measurements, and the second, of 29 July 2005 on the system of digital tachographs. The draft law submitted by the Government to the High House harmonises this subject area and contains in one legal act all the requirements for both generations of tachographs. However, it should be borne in mind that the existing obligation to install only digital tachographs and the resulting expected reduction in the number of vehicles with analogue tachographs installed will gradually reduce the number of analogue tachographs for digital tachographs. It should also be noted that the broadly understood high quality of legislation adopted by Parliament is crucial for the quality of economic trade. I therefore believe that a single law covering all issues, ensuring full consistency of the rules, will affect their readability and compliance with EU rules. This will certainly be good news for the transport industry, especially as the government has declared minimum transposition, and hopefully this is the case. Until now, the adjustment was usually based on the fact that our legislation included those European regulations which were mandatory but also optional. The only way to benefit from this was through bureaucratic offices. However, this is one of the reasons for the absurd overregulation of our economy, with which we fight every day in Modern times. With regard to the detailed provisions, an in-depth discussion should certainly take place during the committee meeting and perhaps the subcommittee, so that the Sejm has the opportunity to hear the position of the social side, as I encourage the PiS club. Ladies and gentlemen! In view of my position on behalf of the Poselski Club I would like to ask for this proposal to be sent to the committee for further work. Thank you.

The grey zone is far worse, but it is not yet the worst, as it will appear in the moment, i.e. simply the situation in which companies, businesses conduct business, sell goods, sell services, pay a certain tax on it, and as a result of such or other activities partially or entirely avoid it, do not pay it or pay less than it should. One such argument is that it is a kind of pan-European God's indulgence, that problems with carousel crimes, VAT fraud are problems that occur throughout Europe, so they had to occur in Poland, and it is difficult to attract to some particular responsibility or to assign special responsibility to Polish governments from that period. So the argument that this is a pan-European act of God, on which Poland could not do anything, falls because most European countries were doing better. ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u496	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Seym! On behalf of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs and the Committee on Justice and Human Rights, I have the honour to present a report on the Government's draft Personal Data Protection Act, print No 2410. The Sejm at its 62nd meeting on 8 May 2018 pursuant to the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm referred the above draft law to the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs and the Committee on Justice and Human Rights for consideration. The Commission for Administration and Home Affairs and the Committee on Justice and Human Rights, after considering this proposal at its meeting on 8 May 2018, request the High Sejm to adopt the draft law submitted. Allow me, as rapporteur, to draw attention to a number of points. In the course of the process on this draft law, substantive, legislative and clarification amendments were introduced. It is about topics that have been the main element of our discussion in the joint committees. I would like to draw attention to Chapter 3, i.e. the conditions and modalities for accreditation, in which the provisions on the activities of the President of the Office for the Protection of Personal Data in relation to the accreditation criteria of applicants for the certification of personal data are further clarified. In particular, it is an article. 12, Art. 13 and Article 13 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of The issue of accreditation is so important that we will have to deal with a number of actors who will try to obtain appropriate accreditations that will give rise to issues relating to the protection of personal data. The second point that has been discussed more widely in committee is the question of changing the name of the President of the Office. Under the new law, we will have a new name for the person responsible for the protection of personal data. It will be the President of the Office for the Protection of Personal Data. Until now, he has been the Chief Data Protection Officer. Here, too, GIODO considered that this change was not justified, but the committee took the view that at the moment the role of this office would be slightly different from that of the old Personal Data Protection Act, and therefore it is appropriate to change the name of the President of the Office. Another issue that we discussed more extensively at the joint committee meeting is the review of the provisions on personal data protection, and I am talking here specifically about the article. 84. Discussions and doubts raised the question of the extent of the rights to be exercised by persons who will monitor compliance with the rules on the protection of personal data. These principles are quite detailed in the article, but Members have had questions on this issue, and on the legal and legislative side all these procedures have been clarified in Article. 84. Finally, the committee decided on the question of liability for breach of personal data protection law - Article. 95 and Article 95 96. I would like to point out that the draft assumes that the court will suspend proceedings before the administrative court in the event that the President of the Office has initiated proceedings for breach of the provisions on personal data protection. Here was a dispute about whether such a decision was correct, but ultimately in the article. 95 and Article 95 96 the committee decided that the court would, as I said, suspend the proceedings in the event that such proceedings were initiated by the President of the Office. Another thing I want to draw attention to is the level of penalties. In this draft law, which we are proposing to you today, the question of penalties or legal sanctions for non-compliance with personal data protection rules is important because the government is in such a position that these penalties will in many cases concern public institutions in which funds come from public danins, so it is difficult to maximise them. Therefore, there will be financial sector units that can be punished for up to 100,000. zł, but they will also be. cultural units - only within 10,000. zł. This is an important issue from the point of view of the functioning of these rules. The last point I want to draw attention to is a whole series of public space problems, monitoring this space. Well, here, as I have already learned today, during the process many institutions have already appeared to the secretariat of the committee with questions, interested in what is the procedure, what is the progress on the draft law, asking also about the issues of monitoring. This is an important issue because many areas in our public space are being monitored today. I would like to point out that, in the part concerning this draft law, we have amended the provisions of the article. 118. It concerns the amendment of the Labour Code, specifies in detail the matter of monitoring at the workplace. It's important. The owners of the workplace will be able to install with appropriate agreement with the employees, with the trade unions monitoring at their premises. The second group, which deals with this issue, which concerns monitoring, is Article. 114, Art. 122 and Article 122 thereof. 123. These articles lay down rules for the use of monitoring by local government units and their subordinate units - these are also important issues - municipalities' offices and their subordinate units, offices of old offices and their subordinate units, as well as voivodship self-government and their subordinate units. Finally, Art. 124 is the processing of data by the Institute of National Remembrance. Here, too, it is important that this issue is regulated by the amending rules. In particular, cases of identification of murdered persons, the publication of the names of those persons, so that there is no doubt on the basis of the Personal Data Protection Act. This article was also adopted in the draft law. Finally, Art. 154 concerns a large area, i.e. monitoring rules for educational institutions. This is because parents, parents' advice, will certainly be interested in this subject. The law in this article sets out in detail the principles and procedures in which this monitoring is to function in educational establishments. Thank you, Mrs Marshal, thank you to all Members, the Minister for the work on this project, and I hope that we will be able to successfully vote on this bill in the course of further work, so that, as far as possible, the draft law can enter into force on 25 May. Thank you very much.

In June Ub. R. Sejm of the Republic of Poland adopted a resolution on the establishment of the year 2018 as the Year of the Bar Confederation. Kazimierz Pułaski in Gorlice, a special place, because it is worth mentioning that Kazimierz Pułaski was one of the commanders and marshals of the Bar Confederation. It should also be added that during the Confederate fights this hero was on Gorlice land, where the battle with the Russian branch of General Drewicz took place in nearby Wysowa. I would also like to stress that the competition is organized together with the branch of the Institute of National Remembrance and the Education Curatorium in Krakow, and this year it was held under the honorary patronage of the Minister of National Education Anna Zalewska.
