ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u522	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On 24 April of this year, the House of Representatives of the United States Congress adopted Act No. S.447, entitled in translation into Polish: Justice for Survivors, who were not compensated, in short JUST. This law creates an extremely dangerous precedent, as it provides grounds for the US government to exert pressure, among others. for countries such as Poland, in the creation of special legal bases enabling the transfer of compensation or equal value of property to persons who did not leave behind heirs and who were murdered during World War II, organisations working to commemorate victims after the Holocaust or to help survivors from the Holocaust. It should be stressed that the case has a history, since the US Senate adopted a twin law on 12 December that year, taking a similar assumption, i.e. such that it will allow, as I said earlier, pressure on certain countries such as Poland to create a special law that will allow for the payment of huge compensation which does not have a basis in our legal system. The amount of these compensations is estimated at between $65 billion and even $300 billion in Poland. It is therefore not only absolutely impossible economically to execute by our state, but, most importantly, this type of claim is absolutely and fundamentally contrary to our legal order, because in our legal order the property of persons who died without child is transferred to the property of the State Treasury and not to any other entities, especially entities and foreign organisations. This case has long been brought to the attention of the American Polonia, who has been taking very intense action from the very beginning to block this action. There was a whole action involving visits to the offices of American Members, offices of senators and not only to convince them that this type of legislation has no legal basis, and the application of it to Poland is absolutely unfair from the point of view of what happened in history. Unfortunately, the activity, actually zero, of the Polish authorities in this area, which are trivializing the matter, is very small. Meanwhile, we know from the debate in the American Congress, and this has been said directly, in an open text, that the purpose of this law is to give the basis for creating pressure, among other things. for Poland to be paid this type of debt, absolutely unfounded, as I said. Therefore, just after the adoption of this act on 24 April this year, the American Polonia took further, very intense action to veto the bill. It was written a special letter addressed to President Donald Trump, in which the American Polonia demands vetoing this law, indicating a number of very specific legal bases, showing that such potential claims have no justification and are contrary to the Polish legal order. There is also a 1960 agreement that has definitely resolved this issue, to a certain extent concerning American citizens, of course. Since I have been working with the United States Polonia on this matter from the beginning, a twin letter - Polish parliamentarians to the President of the United States - was also written with a request to veto this law. The letter was issued until today in the Seym, it was possible to sign this letter. Ladies and gentlemen! High Izbo! Two Members have signed it. I am showing this so that it can be seen how really Polish parliamentarians are interested in this vital issue. Such an important issue of the Polish parliament, Polish parliamentarians show almost zero interest, and it can be extremely dangerous for Poland. So let the Poles know how many people really are involved in this matter and which people are concerned about it. The letter will be submitted to the American embassy tomorrow and published again with the list of people who signed up for it. [[♪ The bell ♪]] I think that Poles will have the opportunity to find out who really cares about Polish interests in this very important context. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u523	Thank you very much. That concludes our parliamentary statements.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u524	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! On Wednesday, 9 May, we celebrate Europe's Day, informally called the celebration of the European Union. It is a celebration established on the anniversary of the presentation of the plan for the creation of the European Coal and Steel Community, known as the Schuman Plan, by Robert Schuman on 9 May 1950. This is an extremely important day in view of the current situation not only on our continent but also on the world. As every year, the values that lead this day are the celebration of peace and unity in Europe. In his speech in Paris in 1950, Robert Schuman, then French Minister for Foreign Affairs, proposed a new form of political cooperation in Europe that would prevent a new war. Its idea was to create a supranational European institution governing all coal and steel production. The Treaty establishing this institution was signed a year later. The Schuman Plan is considered to be the seed of what the European Union is today, as well as the declaration of values that we all now want to confess, namely respect for human dignity, democracy, freedom, unity or, above all, equality. The European Union, in its present form, not only provides us with stability and peace, but also offers extraordinary opportunities for cooperation between countries, economic development and free movement between members of the community. Some of these things are standard for many young Europeans today, and for this reason some do not remember how much we owe to the agreement that we have worked out together over the years. It is therefore worth recalling that the community of European countries is at the same time a guardian of the values that we all now confess and the standard of living that we have today. With this in mind, I would like every citizen of our country to appreciate what we achieved on 1 May 2004, when we became a member of the European Union. Today, 14 years after accession, as every year we can celebrate not only joining the countries of the community, but also taking part in the celebration of the European Union, of which we should be proud.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-09-sejm-62-2.u525	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! May 4th is International Firefighter's Day. In Poland since 2003 Firefighter's Day has been celebrated as a professional holiday, established by the Sejm of the Republic of Poland. The festival was initiated by Australian firefighter J.J. Edmondson of Melbourne to commemorate the tragically deceased five firefighters' colleagues Matt, Stuart, Jason, Garry and Chris - December 2, 1998 in Linton. They tried to save someone's life and property during the fire, as firefighters around the world do. As an expression of respect for fallen firefighters and support for their families, the fire department in the state of Victoria, Melbourne, Australia, consisting mainly of volunteers, and the Community, the state of Victoria, accepted the red and blue ribbon as a symbol. When this information first came to the Internet community, the response was immediate from the U.S. fire crews who accepted to wear the symbol not only on that day. Such a response from across the ocean and letters coming from different parts of the world after the tragedy led J.J. Edmondson to become a symbol of support and respect for all the world's firefighters with the arrival of 1999, and to establish a day where the celebrations could be promoted and coordinated together. After consultation through the Community with national and international firefighters, a date was fixed for the day of St. Floriana in the Catholic Church, patron saint of all firefighters. Officially, the feast was established in 2002 by the Polish Sejm in the amendment of the Law on the State Fire Service (PSP) of 1991. Posted article, article. 30a, formally establishes Firefighter's Day. In Poland, both the State Fire Department and the voluntary fire department celebrates this day. Celebrations related to the celebrations are among others. the opportunity to: nominations for higher officers and aspirants; awards of civilian decorations for outstanding firefighters: For Long Service, for Merit for Firefighters, Deserving for Fire Protection, also for people who specifically supported fire protection activities; presentation of firefighters' equipment and skills. A rare element of the official ceremony is the handing out of the military medal for the Defence of the Country, given by the Minister of National Defence. Following official ceremonies follows the artistic part during which competitions or competitions are held, e.g. between fire units from various cities, in which both firefighters from the State Fire Department and voluntary firefighters participate. A frequent accent is the participation of young people or music bands. Firefighter's Day is a holiday for those who are dangerous, full of dangers, but also in a kind of beautiful service. For can there be anything more beautiful in man's life than giving yourself to others? And that's the kind of ideas firefighters have. These ideas are derived directly from the patron saintly firefighters. Floriana. He who suffered martyrdom as he hurried with his witness of faith, with the help and comfort of persecuted Christians, became the guardian of the professions involving fire in the many dangers that everyday endanger the material and spiritual welfare of man. On this occasion, I would like to thank the bridesmaids and bridesmaids of voluntary firefighters and the officers of the State Fire Department for their rescue activities, their selfless service to another man, for raising young people in the spirit of helping another man and serving their homeland. I wish you the satisfaction of social activity, a lot of health, personal happiness and success in your work.
